#5059. Threshold’s 11-day Bitcoin redemption freeze exposes a hidden risk in BTC-backed tokens

The protocol held more Bitcoin than its outstanding tBTC supply, yet security patches and bridge failures temporarily blocked redemptions.

Threshold Network suspended tBTC redemptions for 11 days despite holding more Bitcoin than tokens in circulation.

The protocol said in a Nov. 18 post-mortem that its app stopped accepting redemption requests on Oct. 30 after unexpected bridge activity raised security concerns. Redemptions resumed Nov. 10 following technical fixes.

The interruption prevented holders from converting tBTC, a Bitcoin-backed token used across decentralized finance (DeFi), into native Bitcoin through Threshold's app. Users could still transfer and trade their existing tokens, while lending platform Aave restricted activity in its tBTC market.

However, the disruption did not appear to stem from insufficient Bitcoin reserves.

Threshold reported that its bridge wallets held 4,320.46 BTC against 4,185.155 tBTC in circulation on Oct. 31, leaving approximately 135 BTC more than the outstanding token supply.

Threshold attributed the interruption to separate technical and security issues that affected its ability to process Bitcoin transactions.

The team had identified a problem with how the bridge accounted for Bitcoin deposits before the suspension. Meanwhile, another security issue forced some node operators responsible for signing Bitcoin transactions to take their systems offline for patches.

Under Threshold's design, a bridge wallet requires signatures from more than half its designated signers to authorize a Bitcoin transaction. This meant withdrawals could not proceed until enough operators returned online.

The protocol also needed to restore Bitcoin transaction proof submissions, which allow the bridge to verify deposits and redemption payouts.

Threshold said it fixed the deposit-accounting issue and upgraded the bridge before its Council restored permission to submit Bitcoin proofs on Nov. 7.

The app subsequently reopened around 03:00 UTC on Nov. 10, allowing holders to initiate redemptions again.

Threshold reauthorized Bitcoin proof submission before it reopened the app to new redemption requests.

Proof submission was reauthorized on Nov. 7. New redemption requests through the app resumed on Nov. 10.

Threshold’s written account gives a signer threshold but does not separately date the return of a signing majority. The timeline represents calendar dates at day precision.

Calendar milestones are from Threshold’s November 18 post-mortem. Parallel tracks depict reported app and proof submission status by date.

Threshold’s written account gives a signer threshold but does not separately date the return of a signing majority. The timeline represents calendar dates at day precision.

The team also reviewed 57 registered tBTC wallet addresses and the bridge contracts for activity between Oct. 25 and Nov. 12.

It matched 31 BTC redemption payouts to their corresponding bridge proofs and reported finding no unexplained Bitcoin outflows in the wallets examined.

Essentially, the findings indicate the interruption was an operational failure rather than a depletion of BTC reserves.

However, the incident exposed a broader vulnerability for Bitcoin-backed assets used in DeFi: even when reserves are sufficient, technical failures can temporarily prevent holders from accessing their underlying BTC.

#5056. Fed's Waller Calls for 50-Basis-Point Rate Hike, Says Tokenized Finance Still Needs Traditional Banks

Fed's Waller Calls for 50-Basis-Point Rate Hike, Says Tokenized Finance Still Needs Traditional Banks

Federal Reserve Governor Christopher Waller wants the central bank to get more aggressive with interest rate hikes, arguing that stubborn inflation may require a 50-basis-point increase in December rather than the smaller move investors expect.

Speaking during a CNBC interview Wednesday, Waller said the Fed must either deliver a half-percentage-point increase at its December 8-9 meeting or explicitly signal that another hike will follow in January.

“You might need to, like, shock people,” Waller said, referring to the central bank's efforts to prevent businesses and consumers from accepting inflation near 3% as normal.

“My view is then you have to go 50 in December to put it in place and get it done,” he said, warning that insufficiently aggressive action could weaken expectations that inflation will return to the Fed's target.

Waller's concerns follow three consecutive months in which core consumer prices rose 0.3%, a pace equivalent to roughly 3.5% inflation annually. That's considerably higher than the Fed's 2% target, which is formally measured using a separate inflation indicator known as the Personal Consumption Expenditures price index.

The governor had already established his threshold for a more aggressive move in a November 10 speech. He said another 0.3% core CPI reading would warrant considering a half-point hike, arguing that moving only 25 basis points could damage the Fed's credibility.

October inflation subsequently met that condition. Headline consumer prices rose 0.4% during the month, while core inflation climbed 0.3%, both exceeding economists' forecasts.

Waller's latest comments suggest he is increasingly concerned about the psychology of inflation, particularly the risk that companies begin treating annual price increases of 3% as acceptable and routinely pass higher costs to customers.

The proposed increase would lift the Fed's benchmark rate from its current range of 3.75%-4% to 4.25%-4.50%. Two consecutive quarter-point hikes in December and January would reach the same destination, but Waller argues that timing and communication matter when inflation expectations are at risk.

Markets remain less convinced that the Fed will move that aggressively in December. Futures pricing Wednesday morning showed roughly a 95% probability of a 25-basis-point increase and only about a 5% probability of a half-point move

The prospect of higher borrowing costs has been particularly painful for crypto investors. Higher interest rates make Treasury securities and other interest-bearing investments more competitive with speculative assets, while tighter financial conditions can reduce the money available for riskier bets.

Bitcoin has already suffered a sharp selloff amid the changing rate outlook. As Decrypt previously covered, persistent inflation fears have coincided with withdrawals from spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds and weakening technical indicators, adding pressure to an already fragile market.

Waller's increasingly hawkish monetary-policy stance comes alongside a more accommodating view of blockchain networks, although he sees established financial infrastructure as essential to its adoption.

In a separate November 17 speech at The Clearing House Annual Conference in New York, Waller discussed how tokenization—the representation of financial assets on digital networks—could reshape payments and securities markets. That speech focused on financial technology rather than interest-rate policy.

He pointed to a growing convergence between traditional markets and blockchain platforms, with banks and payment providers experimenting with tokenized deposits, stablecoins, and programmable transactions.

Waller described how tokenized money could allow investors to purchase securities outside conventional trading hours, with payments and assets changing hands simultaneously through smart contracts.

But he rejected the idea that the development of blockchain-based markets necessarily makes existing payment infrastructure obsolete.

“Central bank money need not be used in every transaction or on every platform, but its presence at the foundation of the system is essential,” Waller said.

#5052. Crypto’s $11 billion bet on Wall Street is running into a funding problem

Traders face sharply different funding costs for identical stock exposures, with Nvidia futures highlighting disparities between Binance and Hyperliquid.

In a Nov. 17 analysis, trading infrastructure firm Talos found that the funding-rate spread for perpetual futures of the same tokenized stock across venues averaged 23% for the 10 largest equity perpetuals by open interest.

Funding is the recurring payment traders exchange on opposite sides of a perpetual future. In Talos’s comparison, rates diverged even when the two contracts tracked the same asset, so the costlier venue one stretch can be cheaper another.

In a snapshot of settled Nvidia funding on Nov. 18, market data tracker PerpDexList showed cumulative settled funding for a long position at 0.3907% of notional over seven days on Binance.

Meanwhile, the equivalent figure for the Nvidia contract on Hyperliquid was 0.3347%.

Over 30 days, Binance longs paid a net 0.5834% and trade.xyz longs on Hyperliquid paid 0.6855%, showing what was paid on each contract before trading fees and any gains or losses on the position.

Cumulative funding paid by longs, as a percent of notional, in a Nov. 18, 2026 snapshot at 18:36 UTC. On Binance and trade.xyz, respectively, seven-day payments were 0.3907% and 0.3347%. Over 30 days they were 0.5834% and 0.6855%.

Source: PerpDexList Nvidia perpetual market snapshot

Binance Research’s Nvidia figures showed a 0.12% funding rate paid by longs on Nov. 16, after three smaller positive settlements on Nov. 12.

Talos puts open interest above $11 billion in November for perpetuals linked to traditional assets such as equities, indexes and commodities. That was about 18% of total open interest across the major venues it studied.

Binance and Hyperliquid together held roughly 77% of that segment, with shares of 39% and 38%, respectively.

To keep perpetual futures prices near the assets they track, venues use recurring payments between traders on either side. When funding is positive, traders betting on a rise pay those betting on a fall, and shorts pay longs when it turns negative.

The exchange can charge a separate trading fee for funding, so the amount transferred depends on the position’s notional value, which can exceed the collateral a trader has put up.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

An eight-hour window lines up the possible four- and eight-hour Binance schedules with hourly settlement on Hyperliquid.

The same holding period can include different numbers of funding payments on different venues.

The markers show timing only. Actual contract schedules and rates should be checked for the period being compared. The illustration does not show which side pays or the size of a payment.

Place scheduled payments at elapsed hours 4 and 8 for Binance’s four-hour schedule, hour 8 for its eight-hour schedule, and hours 1 to 8 for Hyperliquid. Hour zero is the start of an illustrative window.

The markers show timing only. Actual contract schedules and rates should be checked for the period being compared. The illustration does not show which side pays or the size of a payment.

When demand pushes the contract above its reference price on one venue, the premium used to calculate its funding can rise there. A second venue can have a different order balance and a different reference price, even though both contracts track Nvidia.

Under Binance’s TradFi contract rules, funding generally settles every four or eight hours, and the interest-rate input for stock contracts is zero. Outside the underlying market’s hours, Binance can derive the index from prices in its own order book and apply smoothing.

In October, Binance said it would omit disrupted overnight observations from one funding calculation for its US equity perpetuals after connectivity problems.

The Hyperliquid funding rules call for hourly transfers. In the builder-deployed markets that include trade.xyz, the premium compares the midpoint of two order-book prices, measured for a specified trade size, with the market’s reference price, known as an oracle price.

The trade.xyz funding formula applies a multiplier before the hourly payment. Separate books, reference prices, and funding inputs can all contribute to the spread. Talos’s comparison gives the size of the gap, while the exchange rules show how a gap can arise.

A trader could short the contract where funding pays more and go long on the other venue, since holding offsetting positions reduces exposure to stock price moves.

The funding difference can still shrink or reverse before a trader closes the positions, and the two futures prices can move apart while the stock exposure is hedged.

In its Nov. 18 snapshot, PerpDexList put the median cost of opening a $100,000 Nvidia position on trade.xyz at 2.25 basis points over the previous 24 hours, including the taker fee and slippage, the difference between the expected and actual execution price.

A trader also has to account for the other venue, the cost of closing both positions and the possibility that limited depth makes a larger order more expensive. Castle Labs’ venue study measured fees and slippage at multiple trade sizes and sampled weekday and weekend sessions separately for traditional-asset perpetuals.

If one position moves against the trader, gains on the other side sit in a separate account. The losing leg can still be liquidated if its margin falls too low.

A wider funding gap gives traders a reason to take the other side, but they need enough return to carry both positions through those risks.

#5050. Bitcoin ETF assets fall to three-month low as 2026 net outflows top $2.5B

US spot Bitcoin ETFs lost $306.4 million Wednesday, pushing their combined assets to $77.7 billion as Bitcoin briefly traded below $63,000.

US spot Bitcoin exchange-traded funds (ETFs) ended Wednesday with $77.7 billion in net assets, a three-month low, as net outflows for the year topped $2.5 billion.

The funds recorded $306.4 million in net outflows on Wednesday, according to SoSoValue data. The funds held about 1.25 million Bitcoin (BTC) at Wednesday’s close, according to Walet Pilot.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) held the most Bitcoin, with about 779,870 BTC, followed by the Fidelity Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund (FBTC) with 172,080 BTC and Grayscale Bitcoin Trust (GBTC) with 122,730 BTC.

Bitcoin briefly dipped below $63,000 on Wednesday. It traded at $63,545 at publishing time, down 1.7% over the past 24 hours, according to CoinGecko.

Vikram Subburaj, CEO of crypto exchange Giottus, said in a statement shared with Cointelegraph that institutional selling and expectations of tighter US monetary policy continued to weigh on market sentiment.

He also cited onchain data showing that long-term Bitcoin holders had reduced their positions by around 121,151 BTC over 30 days.

Selling pressure also extended to US spot Ether (ETH) ETFs, which recorded around $40 million in outflows Wednesday, bringing their 2026 net withdrawals to roughly $895 million.

#5049. Bitcoin Miners Are Selling Their Stacks to Fund the AI Boom

Bitcoin Miners Are Selling Their Stacks to Fund the AI Boom

Two of the biggest U.S. Bitcoin miners are turning their Bitcoin into AI infrastructure.

MARA sold 13,597 BTC after the end of September at an average price of about $81,039, raising roughly $1.1 billion, according to its third-quarter shareholder letter filed with the SEC.

That’s about 40% of the 34,012 BTC it held at the end of September. Combined with the 26,984 BTC it sold in the first nine months of the year, MARA has sold more than 40,000 BTC in 2026, worth roughly $2.5 billion at today’s prices.

MARA used $750 million of the proceeds to fully repay Bitcoin-backed loans it took out to fund its acquisition of the Long Ridge power plant.

The company said it intends to keep using Bitcoin to raise cash for AI infrastructure, convertible note puts that become exercisable next year and potential share buybacks. Its board has authorized a repurchase of up to $500 million.

MARA insisted the shift is “not a pivot away from bitcoin,” describing mining as its operating foundation while it builds out AI and high-performance computing infrastructure.

Meanwhile, Riot Platforms sold 20,264 BTC in the first nine months of the year for about $1.5 billion, primarily to fund data center development, and ended September with just 2,418 BTC, according to its Nov. 4 results.

The miner also signed a 15-year lease for its entire Corsicana, Texas, campus, covering 910 megawatts of capacity and worth about $28 billion over the initial term. CEO Jason Les called it “the largest lease in Riot’s history.” Riot’s quarterly filing identifies the tenant as Anthropic.

Mining economics help explain the shift. Riot’s Bitcoin mining revenue fell to $116 million in the third quarter from $160.8 million a year earlier, as the average value of each Bitcoin it mined dropped to $70,855 from $114,361.

Including equipment depreciation, Riot said it cost $78,805 to mine each Bitcoin, more than the coin’s average value over the quarter. MARA’s revenue fell 35% to $164.2 million.

The sales add supply to an already fragile market. Onchain analytics firm Glassnode said Tuesday that the two miners drove the recent selling by Bitcoin treasury companies, and that treasury firms outside those two were net buyers in November.

Strategy, the largest corporate holder, kept buying, adding 485 BTC last week to bring its holdings to 850,849 BTC, worth around $54 billion.

#5046. Singapore settles $77.5M tokenized bill sale with wholesale CBDC

MAS issued the bills directly on its test network, which it plans to rename SG Digital Ledger and use for settlement beyond the trials.

The Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS) issued 100 million Singapore dollars ($77.5 million) in tokenized bills on Nov. 6, with banks using wholesale central bank digital currency (CBDC) to pay for them.

Five banks submitted bids totaling more than twice the amount on offer, MAS said Thursday. The four-week bills mature on Dec. 4.

MAS issued the bills directly on the Singapore Dollar Test Network. That gives borrowers an asset they could pledge as collateral for loans on the network, the central bank said.

Singapore began experimenting with blockchain-based bank payments in 2016. In 2025, banks used the test network and MAS-issued digital currency to settle overnight loans.

MAS now plans to turn the Singapore Dollar Test Network, where the bills were issued, into a settlement system for financial institutions, Managing Director Chia Der Jiun said Thursday at the Singapore FinTech Festival. The network will be renamed SG Digital Ledger.

“As the use of tokenized assets and digital money grows, trusted settlement on CBDC will be needed,” Chia said. “It underpins settlement finality and confidence, especially for large value settlement.”

In 2027, MAS plans to issue more tokenized bills, bring more financial institutions into the trials and expand the bills’ use as collateral.

Related: Singapore advances asset tokenization with new MAS frameworks

#5045. Anthropic’s $1.5 trillion IPO exposes bankrupt FTX’s $40 billion missed fortune

Shares once tied to Bankman-Fried, Caroline Ellison and Nishad Singh were sold for approximately $2 billion before the AI company's valuation soared.

Anthropic shares owned by FTX and sold for around $2 billion would now be worth more than $40 billion as the artificial intelligence company finally goes public on Nov. 19.

The difference highlights one of the largest missed windfalls from FTX's bankruptcy, after administrators and US authorities liquidated investments originally made by Sam Bankman-Fried and two former executives.

Anthropic priced its IPO at $83 per share on Nov. 18, valuing the Claude developer at approximately $1.5 trillion ahead of its Nasdaq debut on Thursday. The offering raised a record $81.3 billion.

FTX creditor Sunil Kavuri noted the disparity, calculating that holdings linked to the collapsed exchange are worth more than $40 billion today, compared with about $2 billion recovered through earlier sales.

The investments used funds tied to FTX's multibillion-dollar fraud, which ultimately led to Bankman-Fried's conviction and imprisonment.

Bankman-Fried invested $500 million in Anthropic's Series B fundraising in 2022 through Alameda Research, FTX's sister trading firm, using customer funds diverted from the exchange.

The investment initially gave FTX a 13.56% stake in the AI startup, and subsequent fundraising diluted that ownership to approximately 7.8% by early 2024.

Following FTX's collapse in November 2022, bankruptcy administrators moved to liquidate the investment alongside other assets to fund creditor repayments.

In February 2024, Kavuri and other creditors challenged the proposed sale procedures, arguing that the investment had been purchased with misappropriated customer deposits and questioning whether the bankruptcy estate had the right to sell it.

The court subsequently authorized the sales after a compromise with the objecting customers.

FTX sold approximately 29.5 million Anthropic shares for $884 million in March and disposed of its remaining 15.1 million shares for $452 million in May, generating approximately $1.34 billion.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The transactions represented a substantial return on the original investment. However, Anthropic's subsequent growth dramatically increased the value of those shares.

After adjusting for Anthropic's September 2026 stock split, the 44.5 million shares sold in 2024 would represent approximately 445.4 million shares, worth $37 billion at the IPO price.

The remaining $3.7 billion comes from investments made by former FTX executives Nishad Singh and Caroline Ellison, who invested $40 million and $10 million, respectively, in Anthropic's Series B round.

The US government forfeited its holdings during criminal proceedings.

Anthropic's IPO filing revealed that the company repurchased those shares from the government for about $627 million in October 2025, then resold them to nine investors at the same price.

The Justice Department subsequently transferred the proceeds to the FTX bankruptcy estate for creditor recoveries.

Combined, the two transactions generated approximately $1.96 billion from investments originally totaling $550 million. At Anthropic's IPO price, those holdings would be worth $40.7 billion.

FTX creditors have since received substantial repayments, with major customer classes reaching 105% of their allowed claims by July 2026.

However, those claims were valued in dollars at the time of FTX's November 2022 collapse.

The Anthropic sales provided cash for those repayments, but they also transferred the investment's future upside to new shareholders. Investors who retained the shares purchased from FTX in 2024 would now hold stock worth nearly 28 times their original purchase price.

#5043. Bitcoin Tanks Ahead of Anthropic’s IPO. Should We Really Blame AI?

Bitcoin Tanks Ahead of Anthropic’s IPO. Should We Really Blame AI?

Bitcoin fell toward $63,000 Wednesday, extending a six-week retreat just as AI developer Anthropic prepared to price one of Wall Street’s biggest initial public offerings.

The timing has revived an attractive theory: investors are selling crypto to buy AI shares.

Right now Bitcoin is about to touch $62,000, down 3.8% on today’s session. Anthropic expects to price shares Wednesday at an indicated $70 to $78, ahead of Thursday’s Nasdaq debut under ANT, potentially valuing the company at $1.4 trillion, according to its IPO prospectus.

Kraken chief economist Thomas Perfumo told MarketWatch the IPO “could also be drawing capital out of risk assets” as investors position for the sale.

That’s a theory Binance founder Changpeng Zhao shared, flagging the listing as one possible factor in Bitcoin’s decline in an X post Sunday.

No one knows for sure. Correlation does not prove causation. Here are some potential reasons.

Money shifting to the Anthropic IPO: https://t.co/jYwVi4huAY

ETF outflows: U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs saw about $1.9 billion in outflows over seven trading… pic.twitter.com/XL2aB1ZL9W

— CZ 🔶 BNB (@cz_binance) November 15, 2026

There is a tempting precedent. Bitcoin fell 19% in the 50 days before SpaceX listed in June and 23% in the equivalent period before Anthropic. But the comparison misses an important difference: AI stocks rallied before SpaceX; this time they have been falling alongside crypto.

“This time, the sell-off has been led by the AI trade itself,” Block Scholes research analyst Thahbib Rahman wrote in comments shared with Decrypt. Over the prior 50 days, Nvidia fell 32% and the Nasdaq dropped 11%, unlike their gains ahead of SpaceX’s IPO.

Bitcoin’s 30-day correlation with Nvidia rose from 0.20 to 0.56, while its correlation with gold fell from 0.63 to 0.39. “Liquidity is ebbing and flowing out of crypto and equities together once more,” Rahman wrote.

Bitcoin’s daily chart looks weak without any IPO theory. The relative strength index reads 21.8, well below the 30 threshold commonly associated with oversold markets, while an average directional index of 38.7 indicates a strong trend—in this case, downward.

The 50-day exponential moving average is also below its 200-day counterpart, a bearish configuration associated with the so-called death cross. The pattern historically signals deteriorating momentum and the possibility of further losses, although it is a lagging indicator and does not necessarily predict where prices will go next.

Also, oversold does not necessarily mean the selling has ended.

The immediate floor is around $62,000, close to Sunday’s low. Glassnode data show buyers absorbed 66,000 BTC between $62,000 and $66,000 during heavy selling, but stablecoin supply has shrunk 4.2% over 90 days. Below $62,000, the next psychological level is $60,000; resistance sits near $65,500, $69,300 and $73,700.

More tangible selling pressure has already been recorded. U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs lost $239.9 million Tuesday, including $209.5 million from BlackRock’s IBIT. Nearly $2 billion in leveraged crypto bets were liquidated during the weekend rout, amplifying losses as exchanges forcibly closed positions.

Hilbert Group portfolio manager Jesse Marre called the selloff “simply a stop loss run,” arguing that derivatives traders had already capitulated. Rising Treasury yields, a stronger dollar, and renewed expectations of Federal Reserve rate hikes also make speculative assets less attractive.

In other words, the dip is more natural market moves than money switching markets.

Nvidia forecast $130 billion in fourth-quarter sales, beating expectations after Jensen Huang’s abrupt departure. Yet markets remained split: global shares slipped 0.1%, while the Nasdaq gained 0.35% early and CoreWeave dropped 5.4%.

BitMEX co-founder Arthur Hayes sees the IPO less as a drain than a barometer. “Bitcoin’s going to go up too” if Anthropic soars, he said, while a weak debut could deepen pressure across risk assets.

Anthropic is scheduled to price Wednesday and start trading Thursday. Before that debut, Bitcoin must hold the same $62,000 area buyers defended during Sunday’s liquidation wave.

#5042. Dollar dominance can’t be manufactured

#5039. Why CoinShares Just Sold All Its Ethereum and Half Its Solana

Why CoinShares Just Sold All Its Ethereum and Half Its Solana

CoinShares, one of Europe’s largest digital-asset managers, has sold its entire corporate treasury holding of Ethereum and half of its Solana.

It’s a move that might look like a loss of conviction but is really about something more prosaic: funding a stock buyback.

The decision, disclosed Tuesday in the firm’s third-quarter results, freed up $19.3 million, comprising $16.6 million from the Ethereum sale and $2.7 million from offloading half its Solana.

That covers nearly half of the $40 million share-repurchase program the Jersey-based firm’s board approved in October, and the company was explicit that the proceeds are being “reallocating that capital towards share repurchases.”

CEO Jean-Marie Mognetti framed it as capital discipline rather than a bearish call. “We took the same approach to preparing for the share repurchase program,” he said, noting the sales give the company “the flexibility to act when conditions allow.”

In other words, CoinShares is converting idle treasury crypto into dry powder to buy back its own stock, which it can do under an authorization allowing repurchases of up to 25% of shares outstanding over five years.

The timing is notable. The sale lands during a brutal stretch for crypto markets. Bitcoin was trading around $63,200 on Wednesday, down from six-figure levels earlier in the cycle, while Ethereum sat near $1,770 and Solana around $74, both well off their recent highs.

Spot Ethereum ETFs, meanwhile, have bled cash for days, shedding $29.1 million on Nov. 17 to extend a multi-day outflow streak that has turned Decrypt’s ETF sentiment gauge “bearish.”

Those are precisely the risk-off conditions that have battered Bitcoin treasury firms and pressured the broader sector. For a company whose stock is poised to join the Russell indices in December, deploying capital into buybacks at depressed prices is a conventional play, even if the optics of a crypto manager selling crypto invite a second look.

In terms of scale, these were small corporate treasury positions, not client assets. CoinShares’ total assets under management recovered to $7.4 billion by quarter-end, up from $5.5 billion three months earlier, and its core business of digital-asset investment products is untouched by the treasury move.

The firm actually swung to a strong quarter, posting $39.6 million in net income as net inflows accelerated to $125 million and its lending and trading desks rebounded.

So the sale isn’t CoinShares souring on Ethereum or Solana, assets it still manages billions in on behalf of clients. It’s a treasury-management decision by a newly profitable, soon-to-be-index-listed company choosing to return capital to shareholders rather than let a modest crypto position sit on its balance sheet.

#5035. Visa Direct to extend stablecoin prefunding for cross-border payouts to Asia-Pacific

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Singapore-based dtcpay is testing the service, which lets businesses fund cross-border payouts with stablecoins while recipients receive fiat.

Visa announced plans on Wednesday to let eligible clients in the Asia-Pacific region prefund cross-border payouts using stablecoins through its Visa Direct real-time money movement platform.

Visa is piloting the capability with Singapore-based digital payments firm dtcpay. Recipients receive payments in local currencies and a licensed partner handles the digital asset services, Visa said.

"Stablecoins are becoming an increasingly important part of the infrastructure supporting global money movement. Their long-term value lies in how effectively they connect with the financial systems businesses already use," Vira Platonova, Global Head of Visa Direct, said in the announcement. "Visa is helping bridge traditional financial infrastructure and onchain systems, giving clients trusted and interoperable ways to incorporate emerging forms of value into their existing operations."

In a separate release, stablecoin infrastructure firm Triple-A said it is converting the stablecoins and handling compliance screening and settlement to support the expansion of stablecoin prefunding capabilities in Asia-Pacific, enabling payment service providers to fund Visa Direct outside traditional banking hours.

"Asia Pacific already accounts for nearly a third of global cross-border payment flows, and that share is expected to grow," Eric Barbier, founder and CEO of Triple-A, said in the release. "Yet funding payouts across the region still means navigating multiple currencies, banking systems, and time zones. Our collaboration with Visa brings stablecoins into existing payment infrastructure, giving payment service providers greater flexibility to manage liquidity and fund cross-border payments."

The first two stablecoins Visa tested for prefunding were Circle’s USDC and EURC, a company spokesperson told The Block in September 2025, when Visa announced its initial pilot.

This August, Visa announced a deal with onchain infrastructure provider zerohash, allowing eligible clients to use stablecoins to fund merchant accounts and send payouts in stablecoins.

Last month, Inyo Global went live with USDC prefunding in the U.S., becoming the first Visa Direct processor to offer the service commercially, with Visa now extending this capability into Asia-Pacific.

"Businesses operating across markets and time zones need more flexible ways to move money while keeping the experience simple for the people they serve," said Rhidoi Krishnakumar, Head of Visa Direct, Asia Pacific, Visa. "Stablecoin-enabled funding can help businesses move liquidity more efficiently across borders and optimize treasury and working capital management, while maintaining a familiar fiat payout experience for recipients."

Visa said it will continue working with clients and partners to expand its stablecoin-enabled capabilities.

#5034. IOSCO warns quantum upgrades could slow blockchains, leave security gaps

A Solana test network ran about 90% slower with quantum-resistant signatures, and wallets that keep using older keys could remain vulnerable.

The International Organization of Securities Commissions (IOSCO) warned that upgrading blockchains to withstand quantum attacks could slow transactions and raise fees.

In a report released Thursday, IOSCO said some quantum-resistant digital signatures take up far more space than those used to authorize transactions today. Larger signatures mean fewer transactions can fit in each block, which could increase congestion, push up fees and require more storage.

IOSCO cited an experiment in which a test version of Solana ran about 90% slower with quantum-resistant signatures.

No known quantum computer can break the cryptography that secures these networks today. IOSCO urged firms to start preparing now because the transition could take years.

IOSCO said layer-1 networks use cryptography both to authorize transactions and to reach agreement on which blocks to add. Even after an upgrade, funds in dormant wallets and applications could remain vulnerable if they still rely on older keys.

Layer-2 networks can use quantum-resistant proofs to verify transactions and still depend on vulnerable systems to settle those transactions, move assets between chains or approve upgrades.

In April, Bitcoin advocate Samson Mow warned that larger post-quantum signatures could reduce Bitcoin’s throughput and that rushed fixes might introduce new vulnerabilities.

In October, Starknet’s community backed research into becoming an independent, quantum-resistant layer-1 network, following concerns over its reliance on Ethereum’s security upgrade timeline.

Ethereum researcher Justin Drake has since outlined a 2028 post-quantum transition target, ahead of the network’s existing 2029 roadmap.

Related: AI poses greater threat to Bitcoin than quantum computing, Swan founder says

#5033. Google's Genie 4 Is Here, Letting Ultra Subscribers Build AI Worlds in Real Time

The company announced Wednesday that its latest world model is rolling out through Project Genie, a browser-based experiment for generating interactive environments. Genie 4 was unveiled in October, but access until now had been limited to trusted testers.

Instead of rendering a finished video from a prompt, Genie generates the scene as someone explores it. With the fourth model, users can type instructions midway through a session to change the weather, introduce a character, or steer what's happening without starting over.

Genie 4 also creates sound alongside the visuals, from wind and footsteps to dialogue that reacts to events in the scene. Google says the model improves the way people move and objects respond to physical forces, though those claims come from its own demonstrations rather than independent testing.

Genie has come a long way from the side-scrolling experiments Google showed in 2024. The first version learned from more than 200,000 hours of gameplay footage and could turn a single image into a simple playable 2D environment, as Decrypt reported at the time.

Those early sessions lasted just 60 seconds, and character controls or physical behavior could be unreliable. Google is now keeping Genie 3 available alongside the new model, with sessions extended to three minutes, support for 360-degree panorama images, and the ability to connect multiple generated worlds into longer journeys.

That three-minute limit applies to the updated Genie 3 experience; Google did not specify a comparable session length for Genie 4 in Wednesday's announcement. Users can choose between the models depending on whether they want Genie 4's stronger visuals and audio or Genie 3's longer explorations.

The technology is not the same as a traditional game engine that builds a stable level with fully defined rules. Genie predicts what a world should look and sound like as it changes.

Google acknowledges that objects can drift or change between views, complex physics remain difficult, and generated speech or text may be inaccurate. It says Project Genie's audio and video carry SynthID watermarks and that inputs and outputs undergo safety screening.

Bullish’s adjusted revenue rose 10% to $84.4 million in Q3, and its board authorized up to $300 million in share repurchases.

Crypto exchange Bullish reported a 35% jump in third-quarter adjusted net income on Wednesday. This was largely driven by growth in subscriptions and services, offsetting a weak crypto market.

The company's board also authorized up to $300 million in share repurchases.

Bullish shares Bullish (BLSH) rose roughly 10% to $21.15 on Wednesday morning, leaving the stock down more than 46% year to date.

"We believe our shares are undervalued at current prices," CEO Tom Farley said on the earnings call. Bullish said it plans to file additional financial disclosures with the Securities and Exchange Commission the week of Nov. 30 before it starts any repurchases.

Adjusted net income rose to $18.7 million from $13.8 million a year earlier. Adjusted revenue ticked 10% higher to $84.4 million on the back of a 13% growth in subscriptions and services to $56.3 million and adjusted transaction revenue increasing 5% to $28 million.

Digital asset sales, meanwhile, fell 23% to $32.1 billion.

"The largest unknown for me is the pace of adoption of tokenization technology and when exactly growth will meaningfully accelerate," Farley said.

#5028. Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey Says He’s Building an Offshore Haven for Industry—And 'Illegal' Medicine

Palmer Luckey, the billionaire founder of Oculus and defense technology company Anduril, is spending over a billion dollars to have a private island where he can do things without worrying about U.S. laws.

He wants to build a massive industrial platform in the ocean where American businesses can operate without some of the regulations they face on land. And he has apparently been working on the idea for nearly a decade.

In an X post published Wednesday, Luckey revealed a project called SLAB, a proposed seastead with a 1,000-year design life that he says could support industries ranging from metal processing and oil refining to medical research and treatment.

Selling Oculus to Facebook in 2014 gave me money for helicopter flight lessons and developing a seastead called SLAB with a 1,000 year design life.

The goal was to be cheaper than land and absent state/local laws that cripple critical American industries like metal processing,… pic.twitter.com/aJkd0eOgLF

— Palmer Luckey (@PalmerLuckey) November 18, 2026

His goal, he wrote, is to create an industrial base cheaper than land and free from state and local laws that he believes are crippling critical American industries. Luckey described the project as a way to strengthen the United States rather than escape it, calling it a "relief valve" for outdated regulations.

“Me and a few close friends cashed it up at a $1B valuation back in 2017, and while it still has a long way to go, there has been significant progress towards my goal of reviving manifest destiny and westward expansion,” he wrote.

Luckey said he used proceeds from Facebook's 2014 acquisition of Oculus to finance the project.

The announcement included an AI-generated illustration of a gigantic offshore platform, rather than photographs of an operating facility so it’s hard to know which type of endeavor he’s dedicating his time and money to.

Luckey said he decided to disclose SLAB after learning that journalists were preparing to describe it as a floating AI data center. He rejected that characterization as incomplete, saying the project was considerably older and "way cooler than that."

Luckey provided a more specific indication of his ambitions in a heated exchange with journalist Joshua Topolsky, who questioned whether creating an offshore community was a sensible way to modernize medical research.

Topolsky argued that regulatory reform could happen without constructing an island in the ocean and challenged Luckey to identify which existing rules had prevented him from pursuing medical advances.

Luckey responded that he would rather build a place where people could save their families with "safe but currently illegal medicine ASAP" than wait for lawmakers to reform the system.

He accused political interests and large pharmaceutical companies of obstructing change, arguing that conventional reform would require legislation capable of securing support from 60 senators.

Luckey's plan also faces a significant legal complication: moving an operation into the ocean does not automatically place it outside American jurisdiction.

When another X user asked how close SLAB could operate to San Francisco without falling under its jurisdiction, Luckey answered, "Three miles."

That generally corresponds to the boundary of California's state-controlled coastal waters, according to the U.S. Bureau of Ocean Energy Management.

That said, federal jurisdiction extends considerably farther. The United States has a 12-nautical-mile territorial sea and a 200-nautical-mile exclusive economic zone, where it retains authority over certain economic activities and the construction and use of artificial islands, according to NOAA guidance.

The applicable rules would depend on SLAB's location, whether it floats or is attached to the seabed, and how it is registered. Even vessels operating on the high seas remain subject to applicable maritime laws and the jurisdiction of their flag states, so it’s not as if he’d be living in an anarchist fairy tale.

Medical research presents its own complications, if that’s what he’s actually doing. The FDA requires foreign clinical studies submitted in support of U.S. drug approvals to meet good clinical practice standards, including independent ethical review and informed consent. Conducting treatment offshore would not, by itself, make an unapproved drug legal for sale in the United States.

Previous seasteading experiments have encountered similar jurisdictional disputes. A floating community off Thailand triggered a confrontation with authorities in 2019, while a subsequent project in Panama adopted boat registration to operate within an existing legal framework.

This is all, of course, speculation. Luckey has not publicly identified SLAB's proposed location, the specific regulations it would avoid, or the medical treatments he hopes to make available. He has also not announced a construction deadline or provided independently verifiable details of the project's current physical development.

#5026. CryptoQuant says bitcoin is back in a bearish phase but has reached a ‘value zone’

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Onchain and technical indicators now place bitcoin back in a bearish phase, CryptoQuant said, noting that bitcoin demand growth is contracting at the fastest pace since July.

Bitcoin is back in a bearish phase, CryptoQuant said, noting that it has fallen below its 200-day and 365-day moving averages at $72,900 and $75,900, respectively.

Bitcoin hit a four-month low of about $61,000 on Nov. 15, wiping out the gains from a rally that began on Aug. 19, the onchain analytics firm said in a Tuesday report. Bitcoin is currently trading around $62,500.

"From a technical perspective, trading below both averages places Bitcoin back in the bearish regime," Julio Moreno, CryptoQuant's head of research, wrote in the report. "The averages that acted as support after the way up become resistance after the way back, and Bitcoin will hardly sustain a large rally until it crosses above them again."

Total bitcoin demand growth, including spot and perpetual futures, is contracting at the fastest pace since July 1, CryptoQuant said, noting that it needs to grow for bitcoin to recover and rally sustainably.

Total demand growth has fallen below −400,000 BTC on a 30-day basis, the weakest since the start of July, the firm noted.

"Demand growth has to stop contracting before a durable recovery can start. Price rebounds that are not accompanied by a turn in spot demand have little to stand on," Moreno said. "The first constructive sign would be the 30-day total moving back toward zero, led by spot buying rather than by new leverage in the perpetual futures markets."

Bitcoin holders also realized a net loss of 25,800 BTC during this recent sell-off, the largest daily reading since July 13, CryptoQuant said, noting that realized losses confirm that the decline is being driven by holders selling at a loss, not only by leverage.

Crypto analysts have also turned most bearish since November 22, 2025, with only 58% of analysts remaining bullish on bitcoin, compared to 77% on October 7, according to CryptoQuant's Bitcoin Analyst Consensus Index.

Meanwhile, CryptoQuant's Bull Score Index stands at 10 out of 100, an "extreme bear" phase, indicating "there are no conditions for a sustainable price recovery and rally."

As its price declines, bitcoin has entered a "value zone," as traders' unrealized profit/loss margin has fallen to −20%, the most negative since July 1, according to CryptoQuant.

"A −20% margin says Bitcoin is cheap relative to traders' cost basis, not that the decline is over," Moreno said. "Value zones define where bottoms form, and bottoming formations tend to last at least two months. With demand growth contracting and the Bull Score Index at 10, the conditions for a sustained recovery are not yet in place."

CryptoQuant also pointed to $62,000 to $65,000 as a range where long-term holders sold heavily and new investors bought. The firm said those buyers tend to defend the prices they paid, which could help bitcoin hold that range.

#5022. ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok Helped Plan Terror Attacks in Government Tests

Four of the most widely used AI chatbots helped government investigators develop potentially deadly attack plans in less than 12 hours, despite safeguards intended to stop exactly that kind of request.

OpenAI's ChatGPT, Anthropic's Claude, Google's Gemini and Grok all supplied usable information during covert testing by the Government Accountability Office, according to a report released Wednesday. Investigators posed as attackers, then checked the responses against real locations, available materials, and expert assessments.

Two systems assisted with plans involving improvised explosives in crowded U.S. cities. Two others supplied information for attacks on electrical infrastructure, according to the watchdog, which withheld the models' assignments and operational details to prevent copycat attempts.

Senator Maggie Hassan, the New Hampshire Democrat who requested the investigation, warned that a terrorist could obtain "a concrete playbook for how to carry out a deadly attack" in less than 12 hours, according to her statement.

The chatbots initially activated protections, including refusing requests, removing harmful information or suspending accounts. Investigators bypassed those barriers with common jailbreak techniques, which manipulate an AI system into ignoring its safety rules.

The GAO checked suggested locations, researched whether materials were available and consulted specialists about the plans' feasibility. Some answers contained errors, including misplaced roads on a map, and investigators did not construct explosives or execute an attack.

In the report, "viable" means somebody would likely be able to follow the information supplied, not that an attack was demonstrated to succeed. Each model was tested on one assigned scenario, and the agency said the results were not statistically representative or suitable for ranking the companies.

OpenAI and Google said the tests involved older models, while Anthropic acknowledged that determined users can sometimes extract harmful material. The companies described continuing improvements to safety systems, though the GAO concluded those protections failed in its tests.

This is not really new. The findings follow a previous research showing eight of 10 popular chatbots assisted simulated teenage users planning violent attacks. The separate study tested 720 responses and found the bots provided actionable help roughly three-quarters of the time.

The GAO findings arrive amid outages affecting banks, Microsoft services and the National Security Agency. Officials say the disruptions were separate incidents, not evidence of a coordinated AI-driven cyberattack, even as the rush to repair software flaws has created its own problems.

Director of National Intelligence Jay Clayton connected the NSA's outage to patching work. Officials described the recent breakdowns as maintenance-related, although individual organizations offered different explanations for their failures.

AI tools now find vulnerabilities faster than human teams can test and deploy fixes. Last month, the AI-assisted discovery of critical PHP flaws pushed website operators into an urgent patching campaign; the software powers a large share of the web.

The sheer volume is becoming another problem. VulnCheck data reveals there have been more than 100,000 software vulnerabilities logged this year by November 13, more than twice the total for 2025. Engineers still have to evaluate and deploy fixes without breaking the systems they are meant to protect.

That acceleration cuts both ways. Defenders gain powerful tools for finding weaknesses, but hasty repairs can disrupt essential systems, and malicious users may gain faster access to attack planning.

Hassan said she will make AI safety legislation an early priority when she takes over the Senate Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs Committee in January. She plans hearings examining risks to public safety and critical infrastructure; the GAO itself made no formal policy recommendations.

#5019. Binance obtains $6.5 billion freezing order from Abu Dhabi court in bid to recover stolen crypto

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The Abu Dhabi court orders can be served onchain and provide a legal path for partners to return frozen funds, Binance said.

Crypto exchange operator Binance said it has obtained a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing order from the Abu Dhabi Global Market Courts as it seeks to recover crypto stolen in its Oct. 29 hack.

Justice William Stone SBS KC issued the order against unidentified people accused of carrying out the attack, Binance said Wednesday in a statement shared with The Block. The $6.5 billion is the amount covered by the injunction. Binance previously said about $2.85 billion of the roughly $6.34 billion in assets affected by the hack had been frozen or intercepted.

The court also granted interim orders over specific stolen assets and orders requiring assets to be handed over. Some of the interim orders apply to named defendants that have already frozen stolen assets. Binance does not allege wrongdoing by those parties.

Binance said some issuers, exchanges and other partners require a court order before they can release or return funds they have frozen. The orders can be served via blockchain, and the market will be notified as the list of wallets identified as holding stolen funds is updated.

Law firm Gibson Dunn is representing Binance in its efforts to recover crypto assets stolen in the hack. M. Kendall Day of Gibson Dunn called the $6.5 billion injunction "one of, if not the, largest publicly known freezing orders ever made."

"Alongside the worldwide freezing order against the perpetrators, Gibson Dunn has secured proprietary injunctions and delivery up orders on behalf of Binance to recover what was taken. Just as significant is how the orders will work in practice: they can be served via the blockchain, and the market will be notified of a constantly updating ‘living list’ of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets, enabling those assets to be frozen rapidly even as they are laundered at pace," Day said.

Binance said it continues to work with law enforcement, issuers, exchanges and blockchain intelligence firms to trace and recover the stolen funds.

"We will continue using every lawful tool available to trace, freeze and recover stolen assets, support our partners in returning funds where possible, and keep those assets out of the hands of the attackers," said Dugan Bliss, deputy general counsel at Binance.

Tom Robinson, co-founder and chief scientist at blockchain analytics firm Elliptic, previously told The Block that frozen stablecoins could technically be reissued to Binance, but issuers would first want "a clear legal basis," such as a law enforcement request or a court order confirming the rightful owner. He said that process could take weeks or months. At the time, Robinson estimated that about $3.3 billion remained in bitcoin, ether, and XRP, "which no one can freeze."

Robinson also said tools that trace funds across blockchains help investigators flag them to exchanges and issuers faster. "Each time the funds move, there is a chance to catch them," he said.

#5018. Sweden’s H100 could tap Bitcoin trading pool to fund share buybacks

Shareholders will vote Dec. 7 on whether H100 can buy back up to 10% of its shares, potentially using a Bitcoin pool that lost about 30 BTC in Q3.

Swedish Bitcoin treasury company H100 Group said Thursday it could use Bitcoin from its trading pool to fund share buybacks if shareholders approve the plan next month.

The pool lost about 30 Bitcoin (BTC) in the third quarter and held 233.4 BTC as of Sept. 30, H100 said in its quarterly report. The company held another 3,243.4 BTC in its long-term treasury.

Shareholders will vote Dec. 7 on whether to authorize the board to buy back up to 10% of H100’s shares. H100 would pay shareholders in cash. New Swedish rules allowing companies on the NGM Growth Market to buy back their own shares take effect Dec. 5.

CEO Eirik Grøttum said in September that buybacks could make sense when H100 shares trade below net asset value.

H100 shares (NGM:H100) were up 6.5% at 1.53 Swedish kronor as of 11:45 a.m. Stockholm time Thursday (10:45 UTC), valuing the company at about $170 million. The 3,476.8 BTC it held on Sept. 30 would be worth about $222 million at Thursday’s Bitcoin price of $63,871.

The company reported a third-quarter net profit of 147.9 million kronor ($14.5 million) after recording a preliminary noncash gain of 171 million kronor on its August acquisition of Norwegian Bitcoin company NSD AS. Its operations lost 13.5 million kronor ($1.3 million) in the quarter.

The deal added 2,455 BTC to H100’s holdings. BitcoinTreasuries ranks H100 third among Europe’s publicly traded Bitcoin holders, behind Germany’s Bitcoin Group SE and France’s Capital B.

#5017. Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

Ledger Confirms Tampered Bitcoin Wallets in Berlin as Spy Implant Probe Spreads Beyond Asia

The tampered Ledger wallets aren’t just a Southeast Asia problem anymore.

Ledger said Wednesday it has confirmed tampered hardware wallets in Berlin and is investigating reports of two more in Dubai, expanding a probe it had previously described as a localized incident in Southeast Asia.

“Our findings since then show tampered devices outside Southeast Asia. It is the same incident,” the company said in an update on its support site.

The investigation began last month after customers who bought devices from reseller CryptoBilis reported losses. Onchain investigators traced a suspected drain of about $87 million from hundreds of victim wallets across Ethereum, Tron, and Bitcoin.

Ledger said at the time that all confirmed cases were tied to CryptoBilis, and it later asked resellers in seven Asian markets to pull devices from sale.

Every device Ledger has confirmed shows the same kind of physical modification, designed to capture a user’s recovery phrase during setup. A recovery phrase, typically a string of 24 words, is the master key to a crypto wallet: anyone who has it can drain the funds.

Our investigation into the hardware implant incident we disclosed Oct 15th is ongoing, and we published an update: https://t.co/wx6mfQPr99

There is no flaw in Ledger's secure chip or operating system, and no confirmed case involving a device bought on https://t.co/vQ4QWa1D0f

Ledger maintains there’s no flaw in its secure chip or operating system and no confirmed case involving a device bought on ledger.com.

In Germany, Ledger said a few tampered units were localized to Berlin, and it urged anyone who bought a Ledger device there since January 2026 to contact support.

In Dubai, it’s working with distribution partners to learn how two suspected units entered circulation. Those devices were bought at a Virgin Megastore location, according to multiple reports. Ledger also found one affected Nano Gen5 among the Southeast Asian resellers it previously paused.

The company expects more tampered devices to surface, though it says the number remains small overall. Its Donjon security team is examining suspected devices, and Ledger has asked resellers to pause sales wherever inspections are ongoing.

Customers worried about a device should stop using it, Ledger said. It advises moving funds to a new wallet created on a device bought directly from ledger.com, without reusing the old recovery phrase or passphrase, since a tampered device can capture both. It also recommends filing a report with local authorities.

For a limited time, Ledger is offering physical inspections, including X-rays, at its factory in Vierzon, France.

“We will never ask for your recovery phrase,” the company said on X.

The last few months have been a rough stretch for hardware wallet markers overall. Rival manufacturer Trezor said this week that hackers who breached a third-party shipping partner likely took information tied to about 27,000 orders. That’s the company’s third such breach in as many months.

#5015. Bitcoin Price Rebounds Near $64,000 as Anthropic IPO Lifts Tech Stocks

The bitcoin price climbed back to near $63,700 on Thursday morning, according to Bitcoin Magazine Pro data, as Anthropic’s record initial public offering lifted tech stocks and stock futures ahead of the U.S. open.

Bitcoin dipped near $62,000 late Wednesday after Federal Reserve minutes pointed to more rate hikes. It recovered overnight and briefly touched about $64,000 around 6 a.m. ET before easing slightly. The bitcoin price remains down roughly 11% from a week ago, when it traded above $71,000, and more than 20% below the roughly $82,200 level of a month ago.

Anthropic priced its IPO at $83 per share on Wednesday, above its $70 to $78 range. The company sold about 980 million shares and raised $81.3 billion, topping SpaceX’s record offering in June, CNBC reported. The stock is set to begin trading on the Nasdaq on Thursday under the ticker ANT, with a fully diluted value of about $1.5 trillion.

The pricing sent Nasdaq-100 futures up 2% early Thursday, while S&P 500 futures added 1.1% and Dow futures rose about 299 points. Chipmakers led the move, with Nvidia (NVDA) up 1.5% and Micron Technology (MU) up almost 5% in premarket trading.

Some analysts had argued that investors were selling bitcoin to raise cash for the deal. Others questioned how much that selling actually weighed on the bitcoin price. With pricing now done, the bounce in crypto has tracked the broader rebound in tech. Gray market trading pointed to Anthropic shares opening as much as 24% above the IPO price.

Strategy (MSTR) traded near $93.90 before the bell, up about 4% after closing Wednesday at $90.32, down 6.4%. The company’s shares have fallen sharply alongside the bitcoin price this month, and a New York state pension fund boosted its Strategy stake by more than 10% in the third quarter, a 13F filing showed.

Coinbase (COIN) rose about 3% in premarket trading to near $114 after a nearly 4% drop on Wednesday. Bitcoin miners also moved higher. Riot Platforms (RIOT) gained about 6%, extending Wednesday’s 7% rally, while MARA Holdings (MARA) and IREN (IREN) each climbed about 4% to 5%.

Demand from U.S. spot bitcoin ETFs remains weak. The funds shed a net $306.5 million on Wednesday, led by $148.7 million out of BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust (IBIT) and $69.3 million from Fidelity’s FBTC. The ETFs have posted net outflows on 10 of the last 12 trading days.

The Fed is still the main headwind. Minutes from the October meeting showed that almost all officials judged further rate hikes “would likely be appropriate,” and a couple noted that a larger increase might be needed in December. Policymakers held rates at 3.75% to 4% in October, and Fed Governor Christopher Waller floated a half-point December hike on Wednesday.

Leverage has also been flushed out. About $181 million in bitcoin futures positions were liquidated over the past 24 hours, and roughly 77% of them were longs betting on higher prices. Options traders stayed defensive, with puts accounting for about 70% of bitcoin options volume.

Not every analyst is bearish, though. K33 Research said this week that bitcoin is in “deep value territory” and that “any entry below $70K is a gift from the market” for medium- to long-term investors. The firm noted that bitcoin had fallen below its 200-week moving average near $68,000.

At the time of writing, the bitcoin price is near $63,900.

#5013. Senators: Treasury must ensure ‘equal footing’ for stablecoin issuers in GENIUS Act implementation

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Some states, like New York and Wyoming, have comprehensively developed rules for digital assets and stablecoins over the years.

A bipartisan group of senators is pressing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent to grant a safe harbor for stablecoin issuers operating under state regulatory frameworks, saying without it, state regulators would not be on equal footing with federal regulators.

In a letter to Bessent, Sens. Cynthia Lummis, R-Wyo., Kirsten Gillibrand, D-N.Y., Angela Alsobrooks, D-Md., Pete Ricketts, R-Neb., Kevin Cramer, R-N.D., and Mark Warner, D-Va., voiced their concerns about that disparity as federal agencies work on implementing a law called the Guiding and Establishing National Innovation for U.S. Stablecoins, or GENIUS, which created a federal regulatory framework for stablecoins.

The law allows stablecoin issuers with $10 billion or less to be regulated by states as long as that state's rules are "substantially similar" to what the federal government has in place. The law includes language about a January 2028 deadline where states must submit certifications that they are similar to federal standards. Treasury should also create a safe harbor for state issuers during that transition before the deadline, the lawmakers said, pointing to language in place that allows issuers that are awaiting the federal government's sign-off to get a safe harbor.

"Treasury’s current proposal would offer stablecoin issuers with pending applications to federal regulators a safe harbor as the GENIUS Act requirements come into effect, if such a regulator grants a waiver, without a corresponding protection for state issuers," the lawmakers said.

Some states, like New York and Wyoming, have comprehensively developed rules for digital assets and stablecoins over the years. Those states and others would be considered "substantially similar" to the federal law, they said.

"For Treasury to finalize this proposal in a way that reflects Congressional intent, it must allow issuers to choose from state or federal charters, as contemplated by the GENIUS Act, without the risk of business disruption or uncertainty," the lawmakers added.

Sens. Lummis, Cramer, Alsobrooks and Gillibrand previously wrote to Bessent in June asking for the Treasury Department to preserve states' ability to regulate stablecoins and to set a definitive timeline for stablecoin issuers to be issued by states, creating uncertainty, they said at the time.

Federal agencies are still working to implement the GENIUS Act, with the Treasury's Office of the Comptroller of the Currency the first out of the gate with its final rule set to go into effect in January.

#5011. Russia’s Central Bank to Let Retail Investors Trade Bitcoin on Margin

Russia’s central bank plans to let ordinary investors trade bitcoin and other crypto on margin, capped at 300,000 rubles with a broker, a senior Bank of Russia official said this week, days after retail buying became legal.

Olga Shishlyannikova, director of the central bank’s department of investment financial intermediaries, outlined the plan at the Ural Conference hosted by NAUFOR, the national association of securities market participants, RBC Investments reported. She said the margin rule and two other acts are under review at Russia’s Justice Ministry, and the regulator expects them to be registered by the end of 2026.

It is not yet clear whether the 300,000-ruble margin limit for non-qualified investors will be shared with the existing cap on crypto purchases or counted separately.

The central bank first published draft margin rules in July. Under that draft, brokers could accept cryptocurrencies as collateral for margin positions, and their clients could sell short. Both qualified and non-qualified investors would be able to trade on margin, with retail crypto trades subject to limits set by the Bank of Russia.

The draft also added crypto to the risk-coverage ratios brokers use to police leveraged trades. Those ratios set how far a broker can lend against a client’s portfolio and when positions are forcibly closed.

Shishlyannikova also said the central bank expects the first Russian mutual funds that hold crypto directly to appear in 2027. Professional market participants have already filed applications to register such funds, she said.

“Yes, we are considering the possibility next year of amending the relevant regulations to allow funds intended for qualified investors to buy cryptocurrency,” Shishlyannikova said. The regulator is not considering the same access for funds sold to non-qualified investors, she added.

In late September, the central bank allowed fund managers to invest qualified investors’ money in cash-settled instruments tied to crypto prices, in a directive registered with the Justice Ministry. The proposed change would let those funds hold the coins themselves.

Retail access to crypto futures will take longer. Deputy Governor Philipp Gabunia told reporters on Thursday that the Bank of Russia could let non-qualified investors trade crypto-linked futures once the spot market is running, if it sees no risks, Interfax reported.

“For non-qualified investors, this could be a very sensitive story. Let’s launch, and now we’ll see how it starts to work,” Gabunia said. “If we don’t see risks, we’ll allow it. Non-qualified investors will have to be a little patient.”

Gabunia said the market’s shape could still shift, including which coins are admitted and which derivatives are offered on them. “Legislation is now changing this configuration a lot,” he said.

He said the central bank does not expect “super-large” turnover on Russia’s crypto market. Qualified investors can already trade futures tied to bitcoin, ether, Solana, XRP and Tron on Russian exchanges. Gabunia did not give a timeline for retail access.

Russia’s law on digital currencies took effect Sept. 1, setting up rules for crypto trading through exchanges, brokers and trust managers, plus new crypto exchange offices and digital depositories. Qualified investors were cleared to buy crypto from that date.

The comments fill in the next steps on a calendar that has moved quickly this month. Since Nov. 17, non-qualified investors in Russia have been able to buy bitcoin, ether and Tether’s USDT through brokers and crypto exchange offices, capped at 300,000 rubles a year per intermediary, or roughly $3,600.

A separate central bank ordinance lets exchanges open organized crypto trading from Nov. 22, once the regulator approves their trading rules. Moscow Exchange, which is under U.S. sanctions, has said it plans to list bitcoin on Dec. 1, initially as a pilot.

#5009. Standard Chartered, StraitsX, OCBC complete trial of tokenized deposit interoperability

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The examination tested a shared model where StraitsX’s settlement infrastructure coordinated payments between OCBC's and Standard Chartered's separate tokenized deposit ecosystems.

StraitsX, OCBC and Standard Chartered have completed a proof-of-concept examining interoperability between tokenized deposit networks issued by different banks.

According to the press release, the POC was conducted under the Monetary Authority of Singapore's BLOOM initiative, a public-private collaborative project to develop future payment settlement infrastructure.

The examination tested a shared model where StraitsX's settlement infrastructure coordinated payments between OCBC's and Standard Chartered's separate tokenized deposit ecosystems.

"Tokenized deposits have the potential to enable near-instant and programmable money movement, but their broader utility will depend on how effectively different banking ecosystems can connect," said Tianwei Liu, CEO and co-founder of StraitsX. "We are exploring how shared settlement infrastructure can support that interoperability while reducing the need for bilateral arrangements and prefunded liquidity between individual participants."

The companies pointed out that tokenized deposits currently represent claims solely against their issuing banks and therefore cannot move directly between ecosystems that may use different platforms or standards.

The POC addressed this issue by coordinating payment initiation, compliance screening, liquidity checks, settlement and confirmation across the two banks' systems. This arrangement reduced the need for bilateral prefunding compared with conventional models, the release said.

While tokenized deposits enable around-the-clock deposit transfers, the companies pointed out that different ecosystems need to be aligned to fully unlock that potential.

In controlled tests, the parties completed U.S. dollar payment flows outside standard banking hours, demonstrating that interoperable infrastructure could allow "always-on" value transfer across different ecosystems within minutes.

Any further development, as well as commercial implementation, would require assessment of legal, regulatory, operational and commercial arrangements, including settlement finality, liquidity management, participant responsibilities and financial-crime controls, the companies said.

StraitsX holds a Major Payment Institution license under the MAS and issues the XSGD and XUSD stablecoins. OCBC is Singapore’s longest-established bank and the second-largest financial services group in Southeast Asia by assets.

#5008. Bitcoin Struggles to Hold $64,000 as ETF Outflows Hit $2.9B in November

Bitcoin Struggles to Hold $64,000 as ETF Outflows Hit $2.9B in November

Bitcoin is struggling to hold the $64,000 level as investors continue to pull money from spot Bitcoin ETFs and brace for another interest rate hike from the Federal Reserve.

The leading cryptocurrency was trading at around $63,800 on Thursday, down 1% on the day and 13% over the past week, according to Decrypt data. It has hovered between roughly $63,000 and $65,000 since the weekend, briefly dipping below $63,000 and touching a 24-hour low of $62,068, per CoinGecko data.

"Bitcoin remains under selling pressure," Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com, told Decrypt, noting that it has fallen roughly 26% from its late-September peak near $87,000. "Elevated Treasury yields, dollar strength and expectations of further Fed tightening are weighing on risk appetite, while the broader technology selloff has added to the pressure."

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs recorded $306.4 million in net outflows on Wednesday, after $239.9 million on Tuesday, according to SoSoValue data. The funds have seen outflows on nine of the past 10 trading days, with Monday's small $8 million inflow the only exception, and have lost about $2.9 billion so far in November. Their total net assets have fallen to $77.7 billion. Bitcoin miners have added to the selling, with major miners including Riot and MARA selling some of their holdings to fund a shift into AI infrastructure.

Monday's brief inflow "does not signal a market inflection point," Tim Sun, senior researcher at HashKey, told Decrypt. Net short positions in Bitcoin futures held by leveraged funds on CME have fallen to their lowest level since early 2023, and futures basis yields have narrowed, he said, indicating that hedge funds have largely unwound the basis trade that paired spot ETF purchases with short futures. That means the current outflows are mainly driven by investors actively cutting their Bitcoin exposure, Sun said.

"If macro expectations do not improve, investors may shift from buying the dip to selling into rallies, creating additional overhead resistance on subsequent rebounds," he added.

"Recent Bitcoin ETF outflows and leveraged liquidations" have amplified the decline, Hathorn said. "Despite stronger Nvidia earnings, the market has struggled to stage a significant rebound, suggesting confidence remains fragile. A sustained recovery may require renewed ETF inflows and a more supportive interest-rate outlook."

Some traders have also pointed the finger at Anthropic's upcoming IPO for drawing money away from crypto, though analysts have disputed that the listing is draining crypto liquidity, attributing it instead to ETF outflows, forced selling and rising interest rates.

Traders see a 90.7% chance that the Fed will raise rates by a quarter point at its December 9 meeting, and a 9.3% chance of a half-point hike, according to CME FedWatch. The odds of a half-point move have risen from 6.5% a day earlier. Fed Governor Christopher Waller has called for an even bigger, half-point increase, unless policymakers signal another rise in January.

Hathorn said $64,000 is the immediate level to watch, followed by support around $62,000 and the psychological $60,000 mark. A sustained recovery above $66,000 to $68,000 could ease selling pressure, "but the broader trend remains bearish while lower highs persist," she said.

Onchain analytics firm Glassnode said in its weekly report on Tuesday that the crash had pushed investors’ realized losses to levels seen only near past market lows in 2018, 2020, 2022 and early 2023, a sign of capitulation. Bitcoin is now holding a dense block of recent buyers between $62,000 and $66,000, which grew by about 66,000 BTC during the two days of heaviest selling, Glassnode said. But a daily close below that block could set off a fast drop toward the June low of $58,500, and the Realized Price, the average price at which all Bitcoin last moved, at $53,700.

Traders on Decrypt Predict see a 43% chance that Bitcoin will fall to $60,000 at some point before the end of November, and a 12% chance that it will drop as low as $55,000. They give just a 2% chance that it will climb back to $82,500 this month.

There are some signs of support. About 1.1 million BTC, roughly 5.5% of supply, last changed hands at prices between $60,000 and $64,000, and that band has grown by more than 25,000 BTC in recent days, according to Neil Steinhardt, head of Nexo U.S., in a note shared with Decrypt Wednesday. A dense base of holders with costs near current prices "can act as a support zone, though it is not a floor," he wrote.

Bitcoin has also held up better than gold. The amount of gold one Bitcoin buys rose above its 200-day average this week, to about 17.1 ounces, around 40% above its March low, Steinhardt said.
