#5006. Coinbase Launches U.S. Spot Margin Trading With Up to 10x Leverage

Coinbase (COIN) has opened crypto spot margin trading to U.S. customers, letting eligible traders borrow to go long or short with up to 10x leverage, the exchange announced Wednesday, though access is limited to investors with more than $10 million invested.

“Crypto spot margin is now available in the US for eligible traders,” Coinbase wrote on X. Traders borrow at the moment they place an order, rather than setting up a loan first.

The eligibility bar is high. In a follow-up post, Coinbase said margin trading is open only to people who qualify as “eligible contract participants” under the Commodity Exchange Act. For an individual, that generally means more than $10 million in assets invested on a discretionary basis.

The product is also not available in all states, and the loans are for commercial or business purposes only, the company said. Coinbase did not list eligible states or assets in the posts.

The limit tracks how U.S. law treats leverage. Under the Commodity Exchange Act, leveraged retail commodity trades generally have to run through a regulated futures exchange, so off-exchange margin products like this one are often reserved for eligible contract participants.

Coinbase first described spot margin in October. Eligible traders can borrow against collateral to increase their buying power on spot markets, with leverage of up to 10x on selected major assets and up to 5x on other supported assets.

Collateral can be posted in more than 15 supported assets and stays on Coinbase throughout. Loan balances, collateral levels and margin health appear in real time, and spot positions sit in the same margin portfolio as a trader’s derivatives.

For U.S. customers, Coinbase Credit, Inc. makes the loans and Coinbase, Inc. holds the collateral as custodian. The company said spot margin is not a product of Coinbase Financial Markets, its regulated U.S. futures broker.

The risk disclosures are blunt. Variable-rate interest accrues hourly and can erode a trader’s margin level, and market moves can trigger margin calls or automatic liquidation. In October, the company warned that collateral “may be liquidated without notice” and that traders can lose more than their initial deposit.

Spot margin is one piece of what Coinbase calls its “Everything Exchange.” In the same October post, it said its integration of Deribit was complete and that crypto options, spot margin and unified portfolios would roll out in the coming weeks.

That push leans on guidance the Commodity Futures Trading Commission issued in May, which Coinbase said made Coinbase Financial Markets the only U.S. futures commission merchant able to connect U.S. clients to global crypto derivatives liquidity. The CFTC had already opened the U.S. market to bitcoin perpetual futures that month.

Rivals have moved into the same space. Kraken launched CFTC-regulated bitcoin and crypto perpetual futures for U.S. traders in June, and the CFTC cleared equity perpetual futures for the U.S. market earlier this month.

The launch comes during a rough stretch for the stock. Coinbase shares closed at $110.51 on Wednesday, down 3.9% on the day and about 15% since Nov. 12, as bitcoin slid toward $62,000.

Coinbase has also been adding new ways to trade this month. It launched a ChatGPT plugin for trading bitcoin, stocks and derivatives on Tuesday. The company said in October that U.S. retail access to options is coming later this year, and that Coinbase Pro will return at the end of the year as a platform for high-volume traders.

The $10 million test comes straight from federal commodities law, which defines eligible contract participants. An individual can also qualify with more than $5 million invested if the trade is meant to manage risk.

#5004. Kraken parent Payward to acquire Tribe Capital to expand private-market offerings

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The deal will add private-market sourcing, underwriting, and fund management capabilities to Payward's platform.

Kraken parent company Payward has signed an agreement to acquire Tribe Capital, a venture capital firm and asset manager with $2.7 billion in assets under management.

In a Wednesday statement, Payward said the deal will add private-market sourcing, underwriting, portfolio construction, and fund management capabilities to Payward's platform, expanding the range of investment products available across the company's client base.

Under the deal, Payward plans to develop new private-market offerings through Tribe's existing platform. Tribe is expected to operate within Payward's asset management business, according to the statement.

Tribe Capital, co-founded and chaired by Kraken Co-CEO Arjun Sethi, invests in early- and late-stage private companies across sectors including AI, advanced software, and financial infrastructure.

"Joining Payward gives us the opportunity to bring Tribe's investment approach to a global financial platform," Jonathan Hsu, co-founder of Tribe Capital, said in the statement. "We see a strong fit between our investment approach and Payward's infrastructure, distribution, and product-building capabilities."

The acquisition is expected to close in the first quarter of 2027, subject to customary closing conditions. The company did not disclose the financial terms of the deal.

The Block has reached out to Payward for further information.

Payward continues to expand its offerings through platforms including Kraken and xStocks. Last week, the company added more than 200 tokenized UK and European stocks through its xStocks framework, taking its number of tokenized assets past 1,500.

Payward also announced last week that it plans to introduce prediction markets for U.S. clients and offer equity perpetuals that run on its own exchange, clearinghouse, and broker.

In the third quarter of this year, the company reported $60 million in adjusted EBITDA, up from $23 million in the second quarter, but down 66% year over year, according to the private company's financial highlights released last week. Its total platform margin rose to $487 million in Q3 from $436 million in the previous quarter, but fell 12% year over year.

#5003. GameStop CEO Says Digital-Only Games Have ‘Hurt Consumers’ as GTA 6 Skips Discs

GameStop CEO Says Digital-Only Games Have ‘Hurt Consumers’ as GTA 6 Skips Discs

GameStop CEO Ryan Cohen has criticized video game publishers for abandoning physical discs, as Grand Theft Auto 6, one of the most anticipated games ever made, launches on Thursday as a digital download only.

"When you buy a game, you should be able to sell it, trade it or lend it to a friend," Cohen told Bloomberg. "Unquestionably, the decision to move to all digital has hurt consumers, and it's also hurt GameStop."

Cohen declined to name specific companies, but Take-Two Interactive is releasing the $80 game only as a download, with retailers selling boxes containing download codes. Asked about presales, Cohen said: "It's a code in a box. I mean, we're selling a lot of it."

Take-Two declined to comment to Bloomberg.

The shift hits a core part of GameStop's business. Pre-owned and refurbished products made up 22% of the retailer's sales, about $171 million, in the quarter that ended August 1, down 32% from a year earlier, according to Bloomberg.

Cohen said he has spoken with publishers, who he argued benefit from limiting trade-ins because used copies compete with new sales. "They're taking away competition," he said.

Sony announced in July that it will stop producing physical discs for new PlayStation games in January 2028, a move experts have said will hit low-income gamers hardest. PlayStation founder Ken Kutaragi has dismissed the backlash as a "mentality issue," while an Infinity Ward veteran said publishers have pushed to cut discs since 2007.

Cohen said he has met with Rep. Ritchie Torres (D-NY), who is backing the Power to the Players Act, which would require gaming companies to give consumers ongoing access to the titles they buy. "A game that can be taken away the moment a company pulls the plug is not a purchase. It is a rental disguised as a sale," Torres said in October.

In Europe, the Stop Killing Games campaign gathered 1.3 million signatures for a European Citizens' Initiative calling for publishers to keep games playable after support ends.

The question of whether buyers own digital games has also reached the courts. Sony has argued in court that digital games can't be owned, prompting some in the crypto industry to pitch blockchain-based ownership as a fix.

Cohen's GameStop has also made bets beyond gaming. The company bought 4,710 BTC for more than $500 million in 2025, and in March moved all but one of them into a covered call strategy on Coinbase Prime to generate income. In May, after eBay rejected its takeover offer, GameStop sought shareholder approval to raise its authorized share count to 2.5 billion as it built a stake in the online marketplace.

#5001. Ledger Finds Tampered Hardware Wallets in Berlin, Probes Two in Dubai

Ledger has confirmed tampered hardware wallets in Berlin and is investigating two more in Dubai, the wallet maker said Wednesday, widening a probe into spy implants it first traced to resellers in Southeast Asia in October.

The new cases are “the same incident,” Ledger said in an update on its support site. Every device it has confirmed carries the same physical modification, built to capture a user’s recovery phrase while the wallet is being set up. “There is no flaw in Ledger’s secure chip or operating system, and no confirmed case involving a device bought on ledger.com,” the company wrote on X.

Ledger also found one affected Nano Gen5, its newer model, among the Southeast Asian resellers it had already paused. It did not give a count for Berlin, describing the cases there only as “a few tampered units.”

Ledger asked anyone who bought one of its devices in Berlin since January 2026 to contact its support team. In Dubai, the company said it is working with distribution partners to learn how the two suspected units entered circulation. Its Donjon security team is examining suspected devices, and resellers have been asked to pause sales wherever inspections are still running.

Customers who are worried about a device should stop using it and should not open it themselves, Ledger said. Its advice is to move funds to a new wallet created on a device bought directly from ledger.com, without reusing the old recovery phrase or passphrase, because a tampered unit can capture both.

The company also told affected users to file a report with local authorities. For a limited time, Ledger is offering physical inspections, including X-rays, at its factory in Vierzon, France. “This continues to be a small number of devices overall, and we do expect more to be discovered in our investigation and via social media,” Ledger said.

The probe began in October, after users in Southeast Asia reported drained wallets. Ledger linked the cases to devices sold through CryptoBilis, a Malaysia-based authorized reseller, and asked it to stop selling and shipping Ledger products, BleepingComputer reported.

In an Oct. 15 blog post, Ledger said a “highly organized criminal group” spent months buying genuine devices, fitting them with hidden components and slipping them back into distribution through resellers. “They were patient, they spent millions, and they struck in one coordinated operation, draining wallets,” the company wrote.

Researchers who opened tampered units found a small chip, an eSIM and a cellular module hidden inside the case. The implant read the 24-word recovery phrase off the screen during setup and sent it out over a mobile network. Ledger’s Genuine Check cannot detect an added implant when the original secure chip is intact, the report said.

That is how Ledger can say its chip is sound and still have a problem. Anyone holding the recovery phrase can rebuild every key on the wallet, including bitcoin keys, from anywhere.

Ledger has not put a figure on the losses. One blockchain analytics firm estimated that about $1.68 million was taken from 16 wallets in Dubai and Berlin, after putting Southeast Asian losses at about $102 million across 335 wallets last month.

The breach lands as hardware wallet makers face pressure on several fronts. Trezor said this week that a shipping partner’s breach exposed about 27,000 orders, and BlackRock has said security fears are pushing some whales toward its spot bitcoin ETF.

In October, Ledger told its resellers to keep devices off store shelves and hand them over only at the time of purchase. It also reminded them that returned devices can never be resold.

“We will never ask for your recovery phrase,” the company said on X.

The company confirmed that the new cases share the same kind of physical modification as tampered devices found across Southeast Asia.

Crypto hardware wallet maker Ledger has expanded its investigation into a physical supply-chain tampering campaign to Germany and the United Arab Emirates after identifying suspicious devices in Berlin and receiving loss reports in Dubai.

In a Wednesday update, the company said newly emerged cases were "the same incident" as ones involving tampered devices in Southeast Asia. It explained that it has confirmed the same kind of physical modification designed to capture the recovery phrase during setup.

"There is no flaw in Ledger's secure chip or operating system, and no confirmed case involving a device bought on ledger.com," Ledger stated in its update.

Ledger noted that it has progressed its investigation into reports of "a few tampered units" localized to Berlin, and urged anyone who purchased a device in the city from January to reach out to its support team.

In the UAE, the team has received reports of two tampered units in Dubai, and is investigating how they entered circulation. The company has also identified one case involving a newer model, the Ledger Nano Gen5, which was impacted in Southeast Asia.

Two X users, Rewinskyx and newbabylonorder, claimed to have lost a combined $604,000 in crypto from devices purchased at an authorized reseller in Dubai. The former reported losing about 1,300 SOL Solana (SOL), while the latter said 507,000 USDT Tether (USDT) was drained.

On Wednesday, Bitquery reported that roughly $1.68 million was stolen from 16 wallets in Dubai and Berlin, following the blockchain analytics firm's claim last month that the campaign drained about $102 million from 335 wallets in Southeast Asia.

Ledger has not officially confirmed the amount of losses from the supply chain tampering. The Block has reached out to Ledger for comment.

The expanded probe follows an investigation that began last month in Southeast Asia, where Ledger users reported unauthorized cryptocurrency drains. It was later identified that customers of authorized reseller CryptoBilis were targeted.

In a previous disclosure, Ledger stated that threat actors acquired genuine devices, installed additional custom-built hardware components to transmit recovery phrases externally, and reintroduced the units into reseller channels.

"As we continue our investigation, we expect it is possible that additional devices will be found, and we are working with partners and authorities to find affected devices as quickly as possible," Ledger said in the latest update. "This continues to be a small number of devices overall, and we do expect more to be discovered in our investigation and via social media."

#4997. GTA VI publisher racks up nearly 3x Nvidia’s tokenized stock volume

Tokenized Take-Two shares drew $249.5 million in DEX volume Wednesday, nearly three times the $88.1 million for tokenized Nvidia shares.

Tokenized shares of Grand Theft Auto VI publisher Take-Two Interactive recorded nearly three times the trading volume of tokenized Nvidia shares on decentralized exchanges (DEXs) as the blockbuster began its global rollout.

Trading volume for tokenized Take-Two (TTWO) shares surged 578% to $249.5 million on Wednesday, up from $36.8 million a day earlier, according to Token Terminal data. Tokenized Nvidia (NVDA) recorded $88.1 million in DEX volume despite ranking first by market cap at $399.4 million.

The surge made TTWO the most-traded tokenized stock tracked by Token Terminal that day, accounting for roughly 42% of the $590.7 million in DEX volume.

DEX trading volume by reference stock on Wednesday. Source: Token Terminal

Tokenized stocks are blockchain-based assets that track the value of publicly traded shares, allowing investors to gain exposure through crypto trading platforms. Their onchain trading volumes do not represent transactions in the underlying shares on traditional stock exchanges.

Take-Two shares on the Nasdaq rose 6.2% to close Wednesday at $216.61, according to Yahoo Finance. Shares gave back some of those gains Thursday, falling 4% to $208 in premarket trading as of 7:02 a.m. ET.

GTA VI, which Citi analyst Jason Bazinet estimated cost about $1.4 billion to make, ends a 13-year wait for a sequel to GTA V. PlayStation and Microsoft’s Xbox both said it was the most preordered game in the history of their respective stores.

Most of the tokenized TTWO trading was in a Solana-based asset offered by Backpack Securities, a platform operated by crypto exchange Backpack. The token accounted for about $239 million, or 96%, of the day’s trading volume.

Over the past 30 days, tokenized NVDA shares recorded about $2 billion in DEX trading volume, compared with $808.1 million for tokenized TTWO shares.

The GTA VI launch has also attracted crypto scammers. Blockchain security company Blockaid said in a Wednesday X post that it had flagged more than 8,800 GTA-themed tokens on Solana as malicious, suspicious or spam, alongside hundreds of social media posts promoting fake airdrops and preorders.

Separately, four US senators questioned the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) on Monday about potential gaps in investor protections and anti-money laundering safeguards under its new tokenized securities exemption.

#4995. Global Securities Watchdog Warns of Quantum Computing Threat to Blockchains and Finance

The International Organization of Securities Commissions, the global body for securities regulators, published a 63-page report on Thursday warning that quantum computing is a “significant medium to long-term cybersecurity challenge” for financial markets, and that moving blockchains to quantum-resistant cryptography may be especially hard.

IOSCO said no practical quantum computer capable of compromising widely used cryptography exists today. “The scale and duration of the transition mean the financial sector should start preparing today,” said Jean-Paul Servais, chair of the IOSCO board, in the release. The final report drew on a survey of IOSCO members and interviews with industry experts and market participants.

The group found that preparedness across the financial sector remains at an early stage. Most firms are still raising awareness, setting up governance and taking inventory of the cryptography they use, while migration testing and rollout are limited. Firms cited reliance on third parties, interoperability, limited expertise and unclear timelines as common obstacles.

IOSCO said the problem is sharper for public blockchains because cryptography is built into their core design. Replacing it requires hard forks and coordinated action across thousands of independent validators and node operators, and decentralized networks have no way to force a fast upgrade. The report said that could make any migration “slow and fragmented.”

The report also flagged the cost of the new cryptography. It said signatures from ML-DSA-44, a post-quantum standard from the U.S. National Institute of Standards and Technology, run about 2,420 bytes, roughly 35 to 40 times larger than the 64-byte signatures common today. That could strain throughput, storage and fees. IOSCO cited testing on Solana’s quantum-resistant testnet that showed throughput falling by about 90%.

On exposed coins, the report said a capable attacker could derive private keys from public keys that are already visible on a network, and that uneven migration could leave those assets vulnerable. It singled out dormant or abandoned assets tied to exposed keys, which may be hard to move if the holders cannot be identified or cannot act.

IOSCO did not mention bitcoin by name. The report’s blockchain examples were Ethereum, Solana and Tron.

Timing remains uncertain. IOSCO said conservative estimates put computers capable of breaking today’s cryptography in the 2030–2035 window, but several industry participants argued firms should prepare for the possibility that such machines emerge within the next five years, potentially sped up by artificial intelligence. The report also warned of “harvest now, decrypt later” attacks, in which data collected today is unlocked later.

The report stopped short of setting rules. It urged firms to start migrating to post-quantum cryptography and to build “crypto-agility,” the ability to swap out cryptography without a major redesign. It also told securities regulators to watch for “quantum washing,” where companies overstate what their quantum products can do, and said IOSCO will keep quantum as a priority in 2027.

Bitcoin’s exposure is well documented. Jameson Lopp estimated in May that about 6.93 million BTC, or 34.6% of the supply, sat in outputs with exposed public keys, including old pay-to-public-key coins from Bitcoin’s early years and addresses that have been reused. He also argued in Bitcoin Magazine in September that the debate is about how to minimize property-rights failures, not simply whether to freeze vulnerable coins.

Developers have drafted proposals, but none is active. BIP 360 would add a Taproot-like output type with no exposed public key, and BTQ Technologies has deployed it on a separate test network. Lopp co-authored BIP 361, a draft that would phase out legacy signatures, though he has said the next steps for now do not include it. BIP 360 does not protect coins whose keys are already exposed.

Lopp made the same point about urgency in that essay: “Waiting until a quantum attacker is visible will leave us with the worst set of possible choices.”

#4994. Binance Wins $6.5B Freezing Order Over Hack That Can Be Served via the Blockchain

Binance Wins $6.5B Freezing Order Over Hack That Can Be Served via the Blockchain

Binance has secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing order against the hackers behind last month's attack on the exchange, which the FBI has blamed on North Korea.

The injunction was issued by the courts of the Abu Dhabi Global Market (ADGM) against the unknown perpetrators, Binance's lawyers at Gibson Dunn confirmed, according to City AM.

The order breaks new legal ground. It can be served directly via the blockchain, and market participants will be notified of a "living list" of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets, Gibson Dunn partner Kendall Day said. That will enable "those assets to be frozen rapidly even as they are laundered at pace," Day said.

Binance has also secured proprietary injunctions and delivery-up orders, including against parties holding frozen assets who are not accused of wrongdoing, according to City AM. Decrypt has reached out to Gibson Dunn for comment.

"We will continue using every lawful tool available to trace, freeze and recover stolen assets, support our partners in returning funds where possible, and keep those assets out of the hands of the attackers," Dugan Bliss, Binance's deputy general counsel, said in a statement reported by Bloomberg.

The FBI has attributed the October 29 hack to TraderTraitor, a group linked to North Korea, which has denied involvement. The attackers gained access after an engineer was phished through a fake job offer, according to Bloomberg. The Secret Service has joined the FBI's investigation.

About $2.85 billion in funds connected to the hack has been frozen, including by Ripple and World Liberty Financial, according to Bloomberg. Stablecoin issuers Tether and Circle froze $2.7 billion shortly after the attack, and the Justice Department said last week it had obtained court authorization to seize or restrain $1.2 billion of that.

The hackers have used thousands of wallets and multiple blockchains to launder the rest, and wallets linked to the hack began moving Ethereum again this week after two weeks of inactivity.

Binance has said it fully covered the $6.3 billion in customer assets affected and absorbed the losses itself.

#4991. Sygnum sees bitcoin carry trades beating Treasury bills in 2027 as funding recovers

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Bitcoin funding stayed negative for 67 consecutive days into May before recovering to about 8% annualized by the end of October.

The Swiss bank expects spot-and-short-futures trades to pay more than Treasury bills on average in 2027, and sets a bar of more than two percentage points above the three-month T-bill for the thesis to hold.

Sygnum expects the strategy of buying bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) on the spot market while shorting futures to generate higher average returns than U.S. Treasury bills next year.

Carry strategies combine long spot exposure with short futures contracts. The trade had a difficult run in 2026, when bitcoin's 30-day average funding rate stayed below zero for 67 consecutive days through May.

In its 2027 Sygnal outlook report, the Switzerland-based bank said the annualized basis on CME bitcoin futures had fallen to about 3% by August, below the 3.8% yield on two-year US Treasuries. Hedge funds trading on CME also turned net long for the first time in years as the basis trade stopped paying.

Bitcoin funding rates had recovered to about 8% annualized by the end of October, while yields on sUSDe stood at roughly 5.2%, according to the report.

Sygnum also based its outlook on a potential interest rate hike in the U.S. The bank said fed funds futures imply at least one further 25-basis-point hike through the end of 2027, up from the 3.75% to 4% the Federal Reserve set on Sept. 16.

From the bank's perspective, higher interest rates are likely to raise the return bitcoin carry trades must generate to compete with Treasury bills, while the demand for leverage, alongside wider digital asset adoption, should keep funding rates and futures basis mostly positive next year.

"With advancing digital asset adoption, Bitcoin going from policy to portfolio, tokenization increasing blockchain infrastructure at two speeds, and the AI demand flywheel continuing, we expect demand for leverage to keep Bitcoin funding rates mostly positive through 2027," the bank wrote.

The outlook rests on two indicators: the annualized three-month CME Bitcoin futures basis and the open-interest-weighted average funding rate. Both would need to average more than 2 percentage points above three-month Treasury bill yields in 2027 for Sygnum's forecast to hold.

Meanwhile, the bank would consider its forecast wrong if either average carry or funding returns fell below Treasury bills for two consecutive quarters.

#4989. CLARITY Act Likely Dead Until 2027, Top Crypto Lawmakers Warn

House Financial Services Committee Chairman French Hill said the CLARITY Act is unlikely to pass before the year ends, leaving the crypto market structure bill to a new Congress that Democrats will control.

Hill spoke Thursday at the Capitol Crypto Forum in Washington. “We never give up hope in a lame duck, but it is lame, and therefore the chances are not high,” the Arkansas Republican said. He added that he plans to make market structure legislation his pitch in the next Congress, which begins in January.

“I regret that it was not something we could get done effectively in this Congress,” Hill said. He said 78 Democrats voted for the CLARITY Act in the House and that he expects that support to continue in the new Congress.

Hill also called it encouraging that Rep. Maxine Waters, the committee’s top Democrat, circulated a memo to House Democrats naming digital asset market structure as a topic she intends to work on if she becomes chair.

Sen. Bernie Moreno, the lead sponsor of the CLARITY Act in the Senate, told the same forum there is “no scenario” in which crypto legislation passes the chamber during the lame-duck session. Funding the government, the National Defense Authorization Act and the farm bill will take priority, according to The Hill, and the session is set to end in less than a month.

The Senate blocked the CLARITY Act on Sept. 15, voting 49-50 on a procedural motion that needed 60 votes. All Democrats and four Republicans opposed it, CBS News reported. Sen. Thom Tillis entered a motion to reconsider, which keeps the option of another vote alive, but the bill dies when this Congress ends Jan. 3 unless the lame-duck session revives it.

The House passed the legislation 294-134 in July 2025. President Donald Trump urged lawmakers in August to send it to his desk, but Democrats objected that its ethics provisions did not go far enough to address his potential conflicts of interest in crypto, and regulators have since moved ahead on their own, including an SEC exemption for tokenized stocks.

The industry is now scrambling to repair its relationship with Democrats, Politico reported Thursday. The industry spent $55 million trying to defeat Sen.-elect Sherrod Brown of Ohio, who won his seat back, and Democrats are set to retake both the House and Senate in January.

Sen. Ruben Gallego, a crypto-friendly Arizona Democrat who spent months negotiating the Senate bill, gave Politico’s Jasper Goodman a blunt assessment of the industry’s political prospects. “They’re f***ed,” Gallego said, according to a post from journalist Eleanor Terrett.

Terrett has reported that House Democrats are discussing turning Waters’ Crypto Investor and Consumer Bill of Rights into their own market structure bill. The proposal would give the SEC a bigger role and the CFTC a smaller one, and she cited three industry sources who have spoken with Democratic staffers. Republicans have called it a “messaging bill.”

Hill used the forum to warn about the gap between the two bills. Trump signed the GENIUS Act, the stablecoin law, in July 2025, but market structure legislation never followed. Hill said the SEC and CFTC cannot fill the gap with exemptive relief alone, because it does not give the market lasting legal certainty. “When you pass GENIUS without CLARITY, it’s like passing and creating a cell phone, but you have no cell network,” Hill said.

The stablecoin law takes effect Jan. 18, 2027, and Treasury is still working on its rules. Terrett reported Wednesday that Sens. Cynthia Lummis and Kirsten Gillibrand, joined by four other senators, asked Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent to extend a safe harbor to stablecoin issuers in states with rules in place before Jan. 18, 2028.

#4988. Singapore’s MAS issues $77 million in tokenized bills settled via CBDC

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The central bank said the bills were issued natively on its Singapore Dollar Test Network and settled in wholesale CBDC.

The Monetary Authority of Singapore announced Thursday that it issued S$100 million ($77.5 million) of four-week tokenized MAS Bills on Nov. 6, as the country steps up its tokenization drive.

In a statement released Thursday, the central bank said the bills were issued natively on its Singapore Dollar Test Network and settled in wholesale central bank digital currency (CBDC). The bills will mature on Dec. 4.

Notably, five primary dealers — DBS Bank, HSBC, OCBC, UOB, and Standard Chartered — participated in the auction, which drew applications more than twice the issuance size.

The issuance builds on the 2025 live trial of interbank overnight lending transactions settled via wholesale CBDC. "This paves the way for secured lending transactions involving tokenized assets to take place on the Singapore Dollar Test Network," the MAS said.

The Singapore Dollar Test Network is a shared ledger infrastructure that enables financial institutions to test the settlement of tokenized financial assets using wholesale CBDC, according to the statement. It will be renamed to SG Digital Ledger, the MAS said.

"The live issuance allowed us to test a new method of issuance, and further enhance our in-house distributed ledger infrastructure," said Chia Der Jiun, Managing Director of MAS.

In the next phase, the MAS plans to scale tokenized MAS Bills, expand their use for collateral management, and onboard more institutions.

"MAS aims to demonstrate how tokenized securities can be used more widely in mainstream financial market activities, potentially achieving greater settlement efficiencies in the process," said the central bank.

In an accompanying statement released Thursday, Lim Soon Chong, group head of global transaction services at DBS Bank, said that the initiative has provided "greater confidence" for financial institutions to transact in tokenized assets.

"Over time, more robust infrastructure can help catalyze trading activity and deepen liquidity, supporting the progression of tokenized assets from issuance and experimentation toward more active secondary markets," said Lim.

The issuance comes as Singaporean financial institutions accelerate their adoption of tokenized assets, with MAS' Chia saying that tokenization has "moved beyond experiments."

On Thursday, Standard Chartered announced an expansion of its tokenized deposits solution to enable near real-time transactions across Singapore and Hong Kong. The bank said eligible clients can now utilize commercial bank deposits within digital treasury and investment workflows.

Meanwhile, StraitsX, OCBC, and Standard Chartered said Thursday that they have completed a proof-of-concept testing interoperability between tokenized deposit networks issued by different banks.

Earlier this week, DBS Bank and Kinexys by JPMorgan also launched an interoperability solution designed to enable institutions to make instant, around-the-clock transfers of USD tokenized deposits between the U.S. and Singapore.

#4987. Users are the best investors to have

#4985. Morning Minute: Google’s Newest AI Model Tops Benchmarks Ahead of Anthropic IPO

Morning Minute: Google’s Newest AI Model Tops Benchmarks Ahead of Anthropic IPO

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

Google shipped Gemini 4 Neon on Wednesday, its fourth frontier model release in three weeks, and on the eve of the heavily anticipated Anthropic IPO.

This isn’t just a normal model release. For one, it’s incredibly good. Neon posts 61 on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index against 59 for Claude Opus 5.5 and 57 for GPT-6.2 Sol. It takes the Vals Index at 70.9%, AutomationBench at 56.3% against Opus 5.5’s 42.5%, and GraphWalks long-context retrieval at 95.2% against Opus 5.5’s 66.8%.

Beyond just the outperformance, Google has been releasing at a pace nobody else is matching. It dropped Argon in mid-October. Carbon on October 28. Hydrogen last week. Google owns its silicon, its data centers, and its distribution, and it is using all three to iterate faster than labs that rent compute. And it has some speculating that Google has already achieved RSI (recursive self-improvement), the final step ahead of super intelligence.

So Google has caught up at the top of the AI race. Just in time for the ANT IPO.

Anthropic will be priced at $83, 6.4% above the top of its $70 to $78 range, valuing the company around $1.5 trillion fully diluted. On the 825 million shares in the original terms, that’s roughly $68.5 billion raised, and about $94.5 billion counting the separate $26 billion private placement from its compute suppliers. It trades Thursday on Nasdaq as ANT. Notably, ANT has been trading on Hyperliquid in the $90-$100 range for the past week but popped to $105 this morning.

The valuation math is a 55% step-up from the $965 billion May round and about 25% below the $2 trillion Anthropic was reportedly chasing in September. Revenue ran $33.2 billion through nine months against $2.5 billion a year earlier, a 13x increase. Annualize that and $1.5 trillion is roughly 34 times sales.

That’s a steep raise given the macro backdrop and the Fed calling for more hikes for longer. AI stocks have been shaky over the past month and even moreso since Jensen Huang left Nvidia. Not the most confident market headed into the 2nd $1T+ IPO of the year.

So what does it mean for today’s price action? Personally, I expect a pop to the $95-$105 level that Hyperliquid has been pricing out for weeks, and it likely sees a spike higher. But there will be massive volatility, and it’s very likely to trade under that $83 level over the coming days and weeks. Especially given the new (surprise) headwinds from Google with its outperforming model.

We will find out soon enough. Good luck to everyone trading it today.

#4983. Ant International launches protocol for real-time settlement between tokenized deposits and money market funds

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Ant launched the Whale Orchestration Protocol under Singapore's MAS BLOOM initiative to enable settlement between tokenized deposits and tokenized money market funds.

Singapore-based fintech firm Ant International, which began operating independently following a 2024 reorganization of Ant Group, has launched the Whale Orchestration Protocol, an upgrade to its blockchain-based wholesale settlement platform.

In a Thursday press release, the company said the protocol was developed under the Monetary Authority of Singapore's BLOOM initiative to enable delivery-versus-payment transactions between tokenized deposits and tokenized money market funds.

Specifically, the protocol operates through WhaleRTP, Ant's blockchain-based wholesale settlement platform for real-time treasury and liquidity management. "This unlocks immediate liquidity for the company's real-time global treasury management, allowing idle capital to be deployed in real time," the company said.

Ant International also said it has completed pilot transactions with DBS and Franklin Templeton after the two integrated their respective Treasury Tokens and Tokenized Money Market Fund solutions into its WhaleRTP platform.

"As a full-stack financial solutions provider, 24/7 real-time liquidity management is essential for our business," said Kelvin Li, general manager of platform tech and SVP at Ant International.

Ant International is among the institutions participating in the BLOOM initiative, which the Singaporean central bank launched in October 2025 to develop approaches for settlement using tokenized bank deposits and stablecoins.

The launch comes as Singapore's central bank and major financial institutions step up efforts to develop tokenized financial infrastructure. The MAS announced Thursday that it issued S$100 million ($77.5 million) of four-week tokenized MAS Bills on Nov. 6. The bills were issued natively on its Singapore Dollar Test Network and settled in wholesale CBDC.

Also on Thursday, Standard Chartered announced an expansion of its tokenized deposits solution to enable near real-time transactions across Singapore and Hong Kong.

StraitsX, OCBC, and Standard Chartered also said on the same day that they have completed a trial examining interoperability between tokenized deposit networks issued by different banks.

Earlier this week, DBS Bank and Kinexys by JPMorgan also launched an interoperability solution designed to enable institutions to make instant transfers of USD tokenized deposits between the U.S. and Singapore.

#4982. Swiss Bank Sygnum Says Bitcoin Could Reach New High Near $128,000

Sygnum Bank estimates that bitcoin could reach a new all-time high near $128,000. The Swiss digital asset bank laid out the scenario in its 2027 Sygnal outlook on Thursday, alongside a call that bitcoin and ether will both set new highs while the broader market stays narrow.

The report also frames the current downturn as the shallowest bear market for bitcoin to date. Sygnum puts the drawdown at around 54%, compared with declines of more than 70% in previous cycles, and says digital assets enter 2027 with lower structural volatility and stronger fundamentals.

The outlook arrives as bitcoin trades near $63,000 after six straight weekly losses and a drop below its 200-week moving average. Sygnum’s case is that the market has changed since earlier cycles, not that the slide is over.

Sygnum describes a more mature market. Volatility is increasingly driven by broader dollar liquidity and market structure, it said, and institutions have added new channels through ETFs, leveraged trading and crypto-backed credit. By the end of October, stablecoin supply stood near $298 billion and tokenized real-world assets exceeded $39 billion.

JUST IN: 🇨🇭 Switzerland's Sygnum Bank estimates that Bitcoin could hit a new high of $128,000. 👀Its 2027 outlook also calls this Bitcoin's "shallowest bear market yet". pic.twitter.com/KWyFALBAR8

The estimate rests on a rotation out of technology stocks. Digital assets are worth about 6% of the Nasdaq’s market value, and Sygnum calculates that if 0.75% of Nasdaq value, roughly $360 billion, moved into crypto, total market capitalization could rise by more than 60%.

In that scenario, bitcoin would reach a new high near $128,000. The report does not put a date on it and describes the figure as an estimate built on Sygnum’s own calculations, not a price target. It also notes that a sell-off in AI stocks could spill into crypto. Currently, Anthropic’s record $81 billion IPO is set to open about 25% to 30% above its $83 offering price, which has lifted AI and chip stocks, but worries about higher interest rates have pulled the Nasdaq back from an early 2% gain to about 0.3%.

Behind the price call is a shift in how institutions treat bitcoin. Sygnum predicts it will move out of the tactical sleeve of portfolios and into the “strategic reserve bucket,” competing with gold for capital that wants protection against currency debasement. BlackRock, Morgan Stanley and Bank of America each recommend allocations of 1% to 4%.

The report says 2027 should reward selection over broad market exposure. Sygnum expects adoption to concentrate in stablecoins, tokenized assets, bitcoin as an institutional macro asset and tokens with proven usage, and it believes investors will be rewarded for choosing assets with real usage and revenue.

The math is simple, according to the report. Bitcoin is worth about 6% of gold’s market value, so moving just 1% of gold’s value into it would lift its market capitalization by at least 17%.

Large holders have already shown that appetite. BlackRock has said security concerns are pushing bitcoin whales into its spot ETF, even as miners such as MARA have sold coins to raise cash.

Sygnum also expects tokens that return revenue to holders to outperform, stablecoin settlement at Visa to triple to $60 billion by the fourth quarter of 2027, and carry trades, which pair long spot with short futures, to beat Treasury bills. That comes after the Federal Reserve’s September rate rise to 3.75%–4.00% and 67 straight days of negative bitcoin funding rates in 2026. It describes regulation as clearer but still incomplete, with Europe’s MiCA regime now fully operational and U.S. agencies still advancing rules for crypto markets.

Fabian Dori, Sygnum’s chief investment officer and lead author, said volatility “has not disappeared; it has just migrated from the asset itself to its plumbing,” and that institutional adoption “has kept on building.”

“To understand the shape of 2027 markets, investors must look past price to fundamentals,” Dori said.

#4980. 1inch integrates Hyperliquid’s perpetual futures contracts into its dApp and wallet platform

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Users can fund a perps position with a token they already hold on 1inch, which will be routed to USDC and deposited to a balance on Hyperliquid.

1inch (1inch) announced today that Hyperliquid-powered perpetual futures (perps) are now available to users in eligible regions via the 1inch dApp and Wallet.

The DeFi platform said in a press release that by adding perpetual futures, it seeks to strengthen its offerings to make the ecosystem more holistic. Hyperliquid Hyperliquid (HYPE) markets are available to dApp and Wallet users on Android, with plans to roll out the 1inch Wallet app on iOS in the "next couple of weeks," according to a 1inch spokesperson.

The statement said that 1inch provides the interface to the markets run by Hyperliquid, which cover crypto, stocks, commodities, and foreign exchange. Users can fund a perps position with a token they already hold on 1inch, which will be routed to USDC and then deposited into a balance on Hyperliquid.

"Our goal is clear: whatever users want to do in DeFi, they should be able to do it through 1inch, while maintaining full control of their assets," said Sergej Kunz, 1inch co-founder.

One of the Hyperliquid perps contracts available on 1inch is ANT, which tracks the anticipated price of one Anthropic Class A share as the AI frontier lab prepares to go public later on Thursday.

The 1inch dApp and Wallet on Android allows users to place a market order, a limit order, or a time-weighted average price (TWAP) order. They can attach a take-profit or a stop-loss at entry, or add one later. Users can also add to a position, reduce it, or close it.

The two apps differ in default margin settings. The 1inch Wallet sets isolated margin as the default so that only the position's margin is at risk. The dApp, on the other hand, sets cross-margin as the default, so the balance on Hyperliquid can back open positions.

Since its launch, major DeFi platforms — including Uniswap, Morpho, and Curve — have integrated Hyperliquid's perps market. Kraken parent Payward is also set to be the first CFTC-regulated deployer of Hyperliquid's HIP-3 markets.

According to a recent report from Hyperliquid Research Collective, the derivatives market platform closed out the third quarter with a record $16.8 billion in open derivatives positions, surpassing centralized exchanges such as Bybit, Kraken and OKX in open interest.

#4978. Institutions are Piling Into Bitcoin ETFs, Pushing Holdings to a Record

Institutions returned to U.S. spot bitcoin exchange-traded products as net buyers in the third quarter, pushing their combined holdings to a record 356,491 BTC, according to a new report from Galaxy Research.

Investment managers that file Form 13F with the SEC added the equivalent o f 49,229 BTC, net of sales, between July 1 and Sept. 30. That is the most since the second quarter of 2025 and more than the 47,054 BTC they sold over the previous three quarters. The holdings were worth $29.78 billion at quarter-end prices.

Alex Thorn, Galaxy’s head of firmwide research, published the analysis Thursday. The buying came as bitcoin swung between a bear-market low of $57,717 on July 1 and a high of $87,397 on Sept. 21, and Galaxy said the accumulation continued through that range.

Half of the net addition came from advisors and wealth platforms, a group that includes registered investment advisors, wealth managers, wirehouses and broker-dealer platforms. The category reported 144,307 BTC, or 40.5% of all 13F-reported holdings, the highest share since the funds launched in January 2024.

Those firms have now been net buyers for nine straight quarters. Their holdings are 3.3 times their March 2024 level, and Galaxy said the group accounts for 100,003 of the 179,300 BTC that all filers have added since the second quarter of 2024.

Wirehouses drove the quarter. The category added a record 24,838 BTC, led by Wells Fargo at 14,636 BTC, Bank of America at 7,201 BTC and Morgan Stanley at 2,875 BTC.

Wells Fargo became the largest 13F holder for the first time. It reported 30,553 BTC, worth $2.55 billion, nearly double the 15,931 BTC it held on June 30, and passed Jane Street. No wealth platform had ranked first before, and Millennium Management had held the top spot in every quarter from early 2024 through the first quarter of 2026.

Hedge funds now hold 19.0% of 13F-reported holdings, down from 50.6% at the end of 2024. Basis and relative-value funds hold 34,053 BTC, far below their peak of 101,826 BTC, though they and macro funds were both buyers last quarter, adding 9,184 BTC and 10,492 BTC.

BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust accounts for two-thirds of the institutional position. Filers hold 232,453 BTC in the fund, or 65.2% of everything reported, and IBIT took 34,744 BTC of the quarter’s net 49,229 BTC.

Filers’ holdings of Grayscale’s GBTC fell for the 10th straight quarter, and the lower-fee Grayscale Bitcoin Mini Trust passed it for the first time. The number of managers holding a spot bitcoin fund rose by 191 to 2,166, the first increase after two quarters of decline.

Galaxy noted that a few large firms drove the gain. The 50 largest holders added a net 59,578 BTC, while all other managers combined were net sellers of 10,349 BTC. Form 13F filers hold 27.6% of the bitcoin in the funds, up from 24.8% on June 30.

The filings describe Sept. 30, and the picture has changed since. Galaxy said the funds’ bitcoin holdings have fallen by 38,531 BTC to 1,252,139 BTC as of Nov. 16, while the price of bitcoin dropped to $64,943 from $83,580. Bitcoin had traded above $81,500 as recently as Oct. 28.

A Form 13F covers only long positions at quarter-end and only accounts over which the manager has investment discretion. Short positions and most derivatives are not reported, so shares held by a market maker or basis fund may be hedged. Galaxy also believes client-directed brokerage accounts leave wealth-channel ownership understated.

How filers behaved during the recent decline will not be known until the next round of filings arrives in February.

#4977. US launches cybercrime center with TRM Labs to help trace crypto tied to scams

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The Threat Disruption Center will help investigators share information with payments, crypto, and technology companies to disrupt scams.

The Homeland Security Task Force announced a new unit to disrupt criminal networks behind online scams, with losses tied to cryptocurrency fraud reported to the FBI exceeding $11 billion last year.

Dubbed the Threat Disruption Center, the unit comprises more than 30 federal agencies and over 440 state and local partners with shared intelligence and technology resources to thwart cybercrime.

The task force said investigators will share information with participating companies as they trace funds and identify the networks behind the scams. Exchanges and payment providers can stop transfers or freeze funds, while social media companies can take down accounts used to target victims.

Meanwhile, law enforcement will continue its investigations and pursue court-authorized seizures.

"These partnerships will allow us to identify and dismantle criminal networks faster, disrupt fraud at its source, protect victims, and prevent Americans from becoming the next target," said John A. Condon, acting executive associate director of Homeland Security Investigations.

In its 2025 Internet Crime Report, the FBI recorded 181,565 complaints involving cryptocurrency. Crypto investment fraud accounted for $7.2 billion of the more than $11 billion in reported losses.

The bureau said at the time that many of those schemes were operated by criminal networks in Southeast Asia, some of which use trafficked people to run scam operations.

TRM Labs said its Ardence platform will serve as the center’s primary investigative tool, which investigators will use to process victim complaints, trace cryptocurrency transactions, and map criminal networks.

According to government procurement records, the company was awarded a $94.66 million contract in July to support the operation. The agreement runs through Aug. 29, 2027.

Meanwhile, the new center builds on Operation Level Up, the initiative the FBI launched in 2024 to identify victims of cryptocurrency investment fraud and warn them before they lose more money. The operation has notified more than 8,000 victims and helped prevent over $500 million in losses, according to the bureau.

#4975. Quantum Upgrade Could Be 'Particularly Challenging' for Blockchains: IOSCO

Quantum Upgrade Could Be 'Particularly Challenging' for Blockchains: IOSCO

Financial markets, including crypto, need to start preparing now for quantum computers capable of breaking the cryptography that secures them, according to the world's main forum for securities regulators.

A "sufficiently capable" quantum computer could undermine widely used public-key cryptography that supports critical functions across financial markets, the International Organization of Securities Commissions (IOSCO) said in a report published on Thursday. The cyber readiness challenge posed by quantum computing "is immediate and requires action," it said.

"Practical quantum computers capable of compromising widely used cryptography do not yet exist, but the scale and duration of the transition mean the financial sector should start preparing today," IOSCO Board Chair Jean-Paul Servais said in a statement.

The 63-page report cited conservative estimates that such machines could emerge between 2030 and 2035. Several industry participants consulted by IOSCO went further, arguing that firms should prepare for the possibility that they arrive within five years, potentially accelerated by AI.

IOSCO said the migration may be particularly challenging for blockchains, because cryptography is embedded in their core architecture and any change requires coordinated action across decentralized networks. Public blockchains such as Ethereum, Solana and Tron rely heavily on elliptic curve cryptography to verify identities and validate transactions, and a powerful enough quantum computer could derive private keys from exposed public keys, a scenario known as "Q-Day."

Swapping in quantum-resistant signatures comes at a cost. Signatures under ML-DSA-44, a post-quantum standard from the U.S. National Institute of Standards and Technology, are about 2,420 bytes, roughly 35 to 40 times larger than today's 64-byte elliptic curve signatures, the report said. It cited evidence from a quantum-resistant Solana testnet that saw throughput fall by about 90% when post-quantum signatures replaced existing ones.

Because the cryptography sits in transaction formats and consensus rules, replacing it requires hard forks, and decentralized networks have no way to force quick adoption, making migration "slow and fragmented," IOSCO said. Accounts and smart contracts still controlled by vulnerable keys could stay exposed after a network adds quantum-resistant options, and the path to quantum resistance for Bitcoin wallets remains uphill.

Wallet makers face their own trade-offs. SHRINCS, the leading candidate for a post-quantum Bitcoin signature scheme, offers "stateful" signatures that are about 10 times smaller but can expose funds to theft if keys are reused beyond a set signing budget, Trezor CTO Tomáš Sušánka has said. Trezor is considering making the larger, safer stateless option the default, and hardware wallets will also need post-quantum cryptography for firmware and device authentication, he added.

The report distinguished "on-spend" attacks, in which an attacker races to exploit a public key revealed by a pending transaction, from "at-rest" attacks on keys left exposed for longer. Dormant or abandoned coins pose a particular problem where their holders can't move them. Crypto firms have formed a quantum alliance as some 6 million BTC sit in exposed addresses, and Bitcoin developer Cory Fields has proposed "quantum landmines" to make attacks on such coins costly.

Layer-2 networks are not insulated either. Optimistic rollups could face forged signatures during their dispute windows, typically seven days on Ethereum, and many zero-knowledge rollups use SNARK proofs that rely on elliptic curves. Hash-based STARK proofs are more resistant, but a rollup is only as safe as the layer-1 contracts, bridges and governance keys it depends on, the report said.

Developers are already working on fixes. Researchers have proposed several ways to make Bitcoin quantum-safe, StarkWare has shown a quantum-safe Bitcoin transaction, and Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin has said the network is betting its future on quantum resistance.

IOSCO said firms should migrate to post-quantum cryptography and build "crypto-agility," the ability to update cryptographic systems without major redesign. Quantum key distribution could complement those measures on selected high-security channels, it added.

Most preparations across financial markets are still at an early stage, focused on awareness, governance, training and identifying vulnerable systems, according to the report, which drew on a March 2026 survey of members of IOSCO's Fintech Task Force and discussions with experts. Some respondents described "quantum-procrastination," in which firms put off action while waiting for clearer regulatory signals or wider adoption.

No surveyed jurisdiction reported operational deployment of quantum computing in regulated financial services. The watchdog also urged regulators to act against "quantum washing," in which firms overstate the role, maturity or performance of quantum technology. IOSCO said it will treat quantum as a priority in 2027, focusing on raising awareness across the financial ecosystem.

Quantum hardware is advancing quickly. IBM has claimed a trusted quantum advantage, and Google has said it has cracked some of the field's biggest bottlenecks.

#4974. Ethereum’s new DeFi security tool raises questions over censorship

Phylax’s system lets decentralized protocols block potentially harmful transactions, but raises questions about potential censorship and Ethereum’s concentrated builder market.

Phylax’s new security system could help decentralized finance (DeFi) protocols stop malicious transactions before they settle.

That may bolster crypto’s defenses against North Korean hackers and United States-sanctioned actors, but it may also introduce a way to freeze smart contract functions without explicitly giving anyone the power to do so.

And that’s the kind of control that raises concerns the system could end up as a centralized tool for law enforcement rather than decentralized finance.

“The question is not just whether there is risk, but if it’s acceptable risk,” says Stefanie Boss, co-author of research on censorship resistance and compliance behavior in Ethereum’s consensus mechanism.

“In this case, I’d lean toward that it is acceptable, as the goal is to make the network safer and more aligned with the network and application values,” she says.

Announced on Nov. 10, Phylax’s integration with block builder Titan Builder, allows Ethereum applications to define rules that exclude transactions that may produce harmful outcomes during block construction.

Titan, which Phylax says produces one in every two Ethereum blocks, checks transactions against those rules before including them in its blocks.

The idea is to prevent exploits from settling rather than relying on monitoring systems or emergency interventions after an attack has begun. “Our mission: stop users from losing money to hacks and errors in DeFi,” the announcement said.

Related: Fears of AI-driven DeFi hack epidemic overstated for now — but not for long

In a blog Phylax uses the scenario of a vulnerable vault that could mistakenly release $1 million to a user whose shares are only worth $1,000. With the system in operation, a circuit breaker could cap the amount leaving the vault in a single transaction, preventing the funds from being drained.

Similar safeguards could also potentially limit losses in major hacks like the $6.3 billion Binance hack or the $380 million Bitget exploit, where malicious actors drained exchange wallets and were able to move large amounts of crypto through decentralized protocols.

Phylax says hacks should not settle. Source: Phylax Systems.

While preventing malicious transactions would be a win for the crypto space, digital assets lawyer Gabriel Shapiro warned that applications using the system could become effectively freezable if builders stopped providing the required attestations.

He explained that smart contracts can be configured to reject transactions unless they are included in blocks from builders that have performed the required security checks. If those builders stopped providing attestations, protected functions could keep rejecting transactions until they resumed.

Related: DeFi TVL drops 39% in 2026 amid market downturn and record hack activity

Phylax founder and chief executive Odysseas Lamtzidis says protocols can avoid that risk by allowing transactions to go through even when the system’s security checks are unavailable.

“The sane default we provide and suggest to protocols is a fail-open behavior,” he tells Magazine.

But that safeguard is optional, leaving individual protocols to decide how much priority to give security over availability. “Some protocols might choose to prioritize safety over liveness,” Lamtzidis says.

He also says protocols can decide whether their smart contracts are immutable or can be changed through governance.

Shapiro says fail-open protections should be mandatory and changes to the safeguards should be subject to time delays to prevent protocols from removing them immediately.

There is also a broader concern about whether the system could reinforce Ethereum’s already heavily concentrated block-building market.

Fan Zhang, an Ethereum researcher, says Phylax’s technology is only becoming practical now because of that concentration. “We finally have a way to implement this effectively,” he says, “but sadly only because of how centralized Ethereum’s builder market has become.”

Zhang says Phylax’s approach has “appealing security benefits, especially in the age of AI bots being able to find bugs faster than ever.”

But he warns that introducing additional code at such a critical point in transaction processing creates new failure modes, including implementation errors and builder signing-key theft, loss or impersonation.

“I think the community is very concerned about centralized builders and is making efforts to address them. So I’m concerned that these efforts may undermine each other,” he adds.

Related: FBI blames North Korea for record $6.3B Binance hack

Ethereum researcher Sen Yang tells Magazine, “If major protocols rely on a small number of builders for security enforcement, this could give those builders a competitive advantage and further exacerbate market concentration.”

Phylax announces its circuit breaker system. Source: Phylax Systems

He says Ethereum’s proposed enshrined proposer-builder separation (ePBS) upgrade, slated for inclusion in the upcoming Glamsterdam upgrade, may reduce reliance on third-party relays, “but it does not directly address builder market concentration.’’

Yang says transactions excluded by dominant builders could still be included by non-censoring builders, potentially causing delays rather than permanent exclusion.

Phylax argues that its system does not introduce a new authority over Ethereum transactions because applications themselves define the rules. The technology is “explicitly opt-in,” Lamtzidis says.

He compares the checks to conditions already written into smart contracts. “It’s no different than a ‘require()’ statement,” he says. “Censorship implies arbitrary decision-making or coercion.”

Zhang makes a similar distinction between applications voluntarily filtering transactions and external parties imposing censorship. “Applications willingly signing up for filtering is fine, as a user can walk away from such applications,” he says. “It’s externally forced censorship that’s the problem. That’s where I’ll draw the line.”

Boss says rules targeting a transaction’s outcome are less concerning than those targeting who is making it. “In this case, it is also result-driven, which is often considered less risky,” she says.

She considers the security objective legitimate, arguing that “the harm of those hacks is much higher than the harm of this type of exclusion.”

But she warns that rules could also be used to enforce sanctions or politically motivated exclusions.

“It is important to ensure that there are proper safeguards in place to make sure that these rules will remain fair and designed for security,” she says.

Magazine: Banks are banning AI agents over security... and competition concerns

#4972. Bitcoin finance protocol Hashi goes live on Sui mainnet

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Fluid is taking hBTC for USDC loans, and HoneyB has opened one hBTC vault and one USDC vault on Upshift.

Hashi, Sui's (SUI) bitcoin finance protocol, is now live on mainnet, with its hBTC token available for use as collateral in lending and other onchain products.

Hashi holds deposited BTC Bitcoin (BTC) on the Bitcoin network and mints hBTC on Sui 1:1, according to a statement shared with The Block. Sui said Hashi is decentralized by design and secured by validators rather than a single entity, a model used by some wrapped-bitcoin products.

Hashi previously launched on testnet in July. On Oct. 8, Sui added Anchorage Digital to its list of launch partners and said the group had made more than $500 million in capital commitments, initially intending to roll out the mainnet that month.

More than 20 partners are building markets, products, and access around Hashi, according to Sui, including Anchorage Digital, Fluid, HoneyB, and Upshift.

Decentralized lending protocol Fluid is using hBTC as collateral for USDC loans on Sui, with additional borrowing options expected.

"Bitcoin is the largest pool of idle capital in crypto," Samyak Jain, founder and CEO at Fluid, said. "With Hashi, it can now power deep, capital-efficient credit markets."

Bitcoin yield platform HoneyB has opened two vaults on DeFi vault infrastructure provider Upshift, one for USDC and the other for hBTC. Sui said the strategies may include real-world assets, alternative products, and publicly traded assets. Sui-based vault platform Ember Protocol plans to add its Hashi vaults later this year, according to the statement.

"By connecting custodians, exchanges, and DeFi protocols to professionally managed strategies, we're opening Bitcoin finance to a much wider audience," Upshift co-CEO and co-founder Aya Kantorovich said.

"Bitcoin holders have spent years watching their assets appreciate without putting them to work. We're changing that, bringing real income strategies to BTC and stablecoin holders through Sui," Josh Gordon, CEO at HoneyB developer Nectar Group, added.

Nectar Group raised $5 million at a $30 million valuation last month. Gordon told The Block at the time that its first product would be offered to institutional investors outside the U.S. and target annual returns of about 2% to 4% on bitcoin after costs and fees.

Sui named Blueprint Finance (Concrete), Bluefin, Cetus, Current, and Suilend among the DeFi protocols expected to provide retail access to hBTC lending, stablecoin credit, and yield strategies. Institutions can access hBTC and select Fluid strategies through Porto, Anchorage Digital's self-custody wallet. Cumberland and Wave Digital Assets are expected to provide BTC and stablecoin liquidity, while BitGo access and tri-party settlement are due in early 2027.

Fenwick attorneys argued in an April analysis that Hashi's deposit and redemption mechanics should not constitute taxable events under U.S. federal income tax law, Sui said.

Sui also supports tBTC. Threshold Network added Sui to the networks supported by its tBTC vault tools last November, alongside Ethereum, Arbitrum, Base, and Polygon.

#4970. Gen Z Embraces Crypto While Most Americans Call It a 'Side Quest'

Gen Z Embraces Crypto While Most Americans Call It a 'Side Quest'

For many young Americans, crypto is coming before the 401(k).

Some 21% of Gen Z adults own cryptocurrency, compared with 16% who hold an employer-sponsored retirement plan like a 401(k), according to a survey released Thursday by SoFi Technologies.

That’s seven times the rate of baby boomers, just 3% of whom own crypto. Crypto was Gen Z’s most widely held investment in the survey, ahead of stocks, bonds ,and real estate.

The online survey of 2,064 U.S. adults was conducted by SoFi and YouGov from Oct. 15 to 28. SoFi, a nationally chartered bank that sells crypto alongside checking and investment accounts, developed the report with Culture Co-op.

Gen Z respondents described crypto less as a gamble than a strategy. They were nearly three times as likely as the general population to call it one of the best ways to build future wealth, 21% vs. 8%, and 61% said they see it as a financial advantage unique to their generation.

Only 20% of Gen Z crypto owners said they bought in because it “sounded interesting,” compared with 34% of crypto investors overall. Diversification (32%) and easier international payments (29%) ranked higher.

Part of the gap reflects age. Many respondents in their late teens and 20s don’t yet have access to an employer retirement plan.

Across the broader population, the picture is far cooler. Only 13% of Americans own crypto, and 62% are firm skeptics. Three in four say crypto feels intimidating to get into, and 77% agreed with the statement, “Cryptocurrency feels like a financial side quest that I don’t have the time or energy for.”

Even owners feel out of their depth: 82% of current crypto investors said they should know more about it than they do, and 11% admitted knowing little to nothing.

“Nobody feels the gaps in their financial education more sharply than someone who has just bought Bitcoin,” said Natalie Brunell, host of the Coin Stories podcast and a contributor to SoFi’s crypto newsletter.

The findings echo a recent survey conducted by Ipsos for the DeFi Education Fund, which found that 22% of Americans have ever owned or used crypto but only 13% used it in the past year.

SoFi argues that putting crypto in the same app as banking is the key to wider adoption. Half of respondents open to crypto said having their primary bank offer it would be a tipping point, and 63% of current investors said they’d trade more if they could do it at their bank.

SoFi has been building out that model, launching a tokenized deposit that pays 4.4% and moves like a stablecoin, as well as a bank-issued stablecoin running on Polygon’s payments infrastructure.

The survey also challenges the crypto “tech bro” stereotype. Black and Hispanic respondents own crypto at more than twice the rate of white respondents, 22% to 9%. Miami led the 10 cities surveyed at 20% ownership, while San Francisco matched the national average at 13%.

#4967. Hill, Moreno blame delays for missed Clarity Act window but differ on reach of agency rulemaking

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Moreno says agencies can deliver 80% of a crypto framework, while Hill warns rulemaking cannot replace lasting legislation.

Rep. French Hill and Sen. Bernie Moreno said Congress missed a chance to pass the Clarity Act while momentum from the GENIUS Act was strong, but offered different assessments of how much regulators can accomplish while market structure legislation remains stalled.

Speaking in separate interviews on Thursday at The Hill and The Block's Capitol Crypto Forum, Hill argued that agency action cannot provide the permanent legal certainty that legislation would. Moreno said regulators could deliver much of the framework themselves, though he acknowledged legislation is the ideal route.

"I’m a little bit more bullish than French," Moreno said. "I think it can accomplish 80% through a very rigorous rulemaking process that can’t be unwound."

The Ohio senator added that the Securities and Exchange Commission and Commodity Futures Trading Commission should use the next two years to establish rules that would make it difficult for a future administration to undo.

"Is that the right way to do it? No," he added. "I think the right way to do it is Congress should pass the Clarity Act."

Hill, the Arkansas Republican who also chairs the House Financial Services Committee, said since the Clarity Act's failure, regulators have mostly followed in the footsteps and goals of market structure proposals. But he added that their authority is limited.

"My argument is it’s not a permanent fix. It is subject to litigation. It does not have the force of law," Hill said.

Both Hill and Moreno said it was a mistake to advance the GENIUS Act separately from Clarity, which passed the House but stalled out after a failed Senate vote in September.

"I urged the Senate and the administration last summer to have Clarity and GENIUS passed together in the House," Hill said. "And I still stand by that."

Moreno, a former car dealer, gave a sales analogy for the breakdown.

"Time kills all deals," he said, adding that lawmakers failed to capitalize on momentum from the stablecoin legislation.

"The bill started at 300 pages, ended up at 600 pages," Moreno said.

Neither had much hope for passage during the lame-duck session. Asked what was realistically possible to achieve over the coming weeks, Moreno answered: "Zero. Zero."

Hill was slightly more optimistic, saying lawmakers "never give up hope in a lame duck," though "the chances are not high."

Hill said it was a good sign that Rep. Maxine Waters pointed out digital asset market structure as an issue she intended to address if she's elected as committee chair.

Moreno also said bipartisan negotiations were not off the table, while blaming earlier talks for being "tornadoed" in disputes over President Donald Trump and ethics.

He said he would consider restarting negotiations with the House-passed legislation.

"By the time we got to September with the Senate version, it was just very, very, very complicated," Moreno said.

Hill argued that Congress still needs to finish the job: "To have GENIUS but not market structure is a huge mistake."

#4965. Here’s what happened in crypto today

Need to know what happened in crypto today? Here is the latest news on daily trends and events impacting Bitcoin price, blockchain, DeFi, Web3 and crypto regulation.

Today in crypto, tokenized stocks linked to GTA VI publisher left Nvidia in the rearview mirror. Binance said it secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing order to help recover the stolen assets and broke down the full extent of the cryptocurrencies stolen in the attack.

Tokenized shares of Grand Theft Auto VI publisher Take-Two Interactive recorded nearly three times the trading volume of tokenized Nvidia shares on decentralized exchanges (DEXs) as the blockbuster began its global rollout.

Trading volume for tokenized Take-Two (TTWO) shares surged 578% to $249.5 million on Wednesday, up from $36.8 million a day earlier, according to Token Terminal data. Tokenized Nvidia (NVDA) recorded $88.1 million in DEX volume despite ranking first by market cap at $399.4 million.

The surge made TTWO the most-traded tokenized stock tracked by Token Terminal that day, accounting for roughly 42% of the $590.7 million in DEX volume.

Tokenized stocks are blockchain-based assets that track the value of publicly traded shares, allowing investors to gain exposure through crypto trading platforms. Their onchain trading volumes do not represent transactions in the underlying shares on traditional stock exchanges.

Take-Two shares on the Nasdaq rose 6.2% to close Wednesday at $216.61, according to Yahoo Finance. Shares gave back some of those gains Thursday, falling 4% to $208 in premarket trading as of 7:02 a.m. ET.

GTA VI, which Citi analyst Jason Bazinet estimated cost about $1.4 billion to make, ends a 13-year wait for a sequel to GTA V. PlayStation and Microsoft’s Xbox both said it was the most preordered game in the history of their respective stores.

Hackers behind the record $6.3 billion Binance hack stole a total of 192 cryptocurrencies across 11 blockchain networks, new court documents reveal.

The figures come from an asset schedule submitted by Nest Clearing and Custody Limited, which operated the affected Binance wallets, and included in a Wednesday Abu Dhabi court order shared with Cointelegraph.

Tether’s USDt (USDT) accounted for the largest share of stolen value at $2.50 billion, followed by $1.42 billion in Bitcoin (BTC) and $826 million in Circle’s USDC (USDC). Together, the three assets made up about 76% of the total value of misappropriated tokens.

While earlier reports identified many of the tokens stolen in the attack, the latest court documents show a full breakdown of all crypto assets stolen in the attack, including the quantities and what they were worth at the time of the theft.

Binance said it has secured a $6.5 billion worldwide freezing injunction from the Abu Dhabi Global Market Courts as it seeks to recover assets stolen in its record Oct. 29 hack.

The court orders target unidentified perpetrators of the attack and are intended to help issuers, exchanges and other partners freeze and return stolen assets, the company said in a statement shared with Cointelegraph on Wednesday.

The orders can be served via blockchain, according to M. Kendall Day of Gibson Dunn, which represents Binance. This allows legal notices to be delivered to wallet addresses rather than through traditional channels. Day said market participants would also be notified of updates to a “living list” of 26,000 wallet addresses identified as holding stolen assets.

Binance said some businesses require formal court orders before they can release, transfer or return frozen funds. The injunction’s $6.5 billion amount is not a tally of recovered assets, and the statement did not disclose how much had so far been returned under the orders.

Binance also said it obtained orders requiring stolen assets to be handed over. Separate interim orders cover assets frozen by certain named parties against whom no wrongdoing is alleged.

#4964. Tokenized Stocks Shrug Off the Crypto Selloff

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group
